# T H E
# GOLDEN LION,
### or the
## Elixir of the Wise.
<note>original: 'Afijn' (an alchemical term for an elixir or essence)</note>

**In which countless wonderful Arts** and useful Secrets are revealed: such as extracting the *Anima* <note>original: 'Anima' (the "soul" or spiritual essence of a substance in alchemy)</note> from all Metals and Minerals; to make many uncommon Medicines, Painter’s Gold <note>fine gold powder or imitation gold used in illumination and art</note>, Brandies from Grain without a foul taste, excellent Cosmetic Whiteners <note>original: 'Blancketsel' (a lead- or bismuth-based skin-whitening powder common in the 17th century)</note>, precious Gemstones, etc.

*All wrought with one's own hands, and brought to light with many copper Plates,*

->*Mining Master <note>a title indicating a high-ranking official or expert in metallurgy and mining operations</note>.*<-

### to the

# R E A D E R.

<image-desc>Large ornamental drop cap 'G' featuring floral and vine motifs within a rectangular frame.</image-desc>

Kind Reader. I wish to share with you once more something of the most noble <term>Art<note>original: 'Konst'; in alchemical literature, 'the Art' refers to the Great Work (Magnum Opus) of transforming matter and the soul.</note></term>, or <term>Hermetic Philosophy<note>A philosophical and spiritual tradition based on writings attributed to Hermes Trismegistus, focusing on the hidden laws of nature.</note></term>, the substance of which consists in **Wine** and **Salt**, about which so many books have been written; yet before we obtain this Salt, and drink the Wine that Saturn <note>original: '♄'; the alchemical symbol for Saturn, which represents the metal lead and the 'nigredo' or blackening stage of the alchemical process.</note> has drunk, one must first learn to know well the four seasons of the year, as well as the **East** and the **South** wind.

When we then wish to imitate Nature, we must rightly observe these aforementioned things; and once we have found how to understand the four seasons of the year, to separate the Elements from one another, and [to observe] the four **Winds** closely

It happened to me that, as a favor to someone, I wished to share the knowledge of this work; however, I was answered that they had already done much in Alchemy, and knew well how they should conduct themselves in this Art; but they knew neither the Wine, nor the <term>Mercury ☿</term> <note>original symbol: ☿. To return to natural things: when the Wine is still in the grape and not yet pressed out, it still wears its raw garments; but when it is cut from the vines and pressed out, the Wine acquires a different nature, both in scent and in taste. [vessels] or tuns, and afterwards, through fermentation, it acquires another Nature, because then a separation occurs between body and spirit; for <term>Tartar</term> <note>original: "Wijnsteen." Potassium bitartrate, a sediment that forms during wine fermentation. In alchemy, this precipitate was considered the "body" or salt of the liquid.</note> is the body of the Wine.

Nature would not have been able to separate this on its own if the Farmers had not helped Nature: therefore, a Farmer in that work may well be considered a <term>Philosopher</term> <note>The author uses "Philosopher" in the sense of a "Natural Philosopher" or Alchemist—someone who understands the hidden workings of the natural world.</note>, as they follow Nature better than we do: for they first do their work, and then let Nature work of itself. If we then wish to follow Nature, let us do no differently than Nature. explanation regarding the <term>12 Keys</term> <note>Referring to *The Twelve Keys of Basil Valentine*, a famous alchemical text published in the late 16th century that describes the stages of the alchemical Great Work.</note>, page 202.

# PREFACE

similar names. From this it is clear enough to see what powers reside in our <term>Antimony</term> <note>symbol: ⚹. Whoever then wishes to work in this <term>Art</term> must first learn to separate the <term>Elements</term>; not through the violence of <term>Fire</term> <note>symbol: △. The bodies of the <term>Metals</term> are salt; they must be made into salt by <term>Metals</term>.

<meta>This page continues the preface begun on the previous page, moving from the specific properties of Antimony and Saturn to the foundational philosophy of the "Art," emphasizing that alchemical work must mirror the natural processes of creation as described in the Holy Scriptures.</meta>

# PREFACE.

This Art is learned in no other way than through Nature, and with **Nature**, by which everything is wrought. For this reason, Hermes <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary Hellenistic figure regarded as the father of alchemy (the "Hermetic Art").</note> held this Art in such high honor, just as it is still honored by all sincere practitioners <note>original: "Laboranten"</note>, and is considered the most glorious Philosophy; because everything within it is wrought with Nature, which has its beginning with <term>Water</term> <note>symbol: ∇</note> and <term>Fire</term> <note>symbol: Δ</note>; and this was the first operation of Nature, or through Nature, or in Nature, of which enough can be read in the Holy Scripture <note>A reference to the Book of Genesis, where the Spirit of God moved over the waters and light (fire) was created.</note>.

Therefore, seek the <term>Fire</term> <note>symbol: Δ</note> that God made, and the <term>Water</term> <note>symbol: ∇</note> with which it may be coagulated <note>To "coagulate" is to turn a liquid into a solid or semi-solid state; in alchemy, it is the process of fixing a volatile spirit into a stable body.</note>; and that <term>Water</term> and <term>Fire</term> is the salt, which I have so often called by the name of Wine and Lead—namely ours <note>The author distinguishes between common materials and "Philosophical" Wine or Lead, which are specialized substances prepared for the Work.</note>—from which one makes such salt. I pray as a friend to all those who shall read this, to bear with me <note>Dutch: "my ten besten te houden," an idiom asking the reader to interpret his repetition favorably.</note> that I speak so often here of this Wine and Metals; for I do this for those who

[Sa]turn concludes, whom I once saw in various garments; and on his head he had a green hat; and around that hat was a red cord; and he had a gray beard, with red feet: and this one came to me in the winter. # THE GOLDEN LION,

->Or the<-

### VINEGAR of the SAGES.

IN the beginning, when God the Lord created Heaven and Earth through His Word, the Spirit of the Lord hovered over the waters; but after the waters were divided, the Dry Land appeared, so that the Earth might bear fruits according to its nature, empowered by the Spirit of God, which previously hovered over the water and makes everything grow. This growing power is attributed to our <term>Vitriol</term> <note>In alchemy, Vitriol is not merely a chemical salt (like iron sulfate), but is often used as a code for the "Universal Solvent." It was frequently explained through the Latin acronym *V.I.T.R.I.O.L.*: "Visit the interior of the earth; by rectification thou shalt find the hidden stone."</note>, or *Vinegar of the Sages* <note>original: 'Acetum der Wisen'. A philosophical acidic liquid used to dissolve or "open" metals so their inner essence could be extracted.</note>; and this is nothing other than the greenness of the Earth <note>The "greenness" (Viriditas) or "Green Lion" represents the raw, vital life-force of nature. Alchemists believed this energy was the key to transformation.</note>, which we can see daily with our eyes, and which is enjoyed by mankind for the maintenance of our bodies, for which we must thank God Almighty every day, as long as Heaven and Earth shall stand.

beginning made everything out of <term>Earth</term> <note>original: ▽. This is the alchemical symbol for the element of Earth.</note>, as the words of the Holy Scripture clearly seem to testify. Thereafter God separated the waters from the Earth, so that the Earth would bring forth fruits. But as long as the Earth was covered with water, the darkness still had the upper hand, swallowing everything up; so that the greenness of the Earth was merely black and stinking to nature.

When God then had made the light and driven away the darkness, then Justice <note>In this context, 'Justice' refers to a state of divine order or the purification of matter.</note> came forth, draped in white garments, which reveals to us where the greenness is hidden: in the bowels of this white Earth; without which greenness, nothing on Earth can grow. If we were to have no Winter, then everything would perish and come to nothing: for the Winter is a master of the green nature, serving to renew it and to bring it again to greater powers. This is then called the <term>Vitriol of the Sages</term>, or the <term>Salt of Heaven</term>, of which so many excellent books have been written: for if one cannot make this Vitriol white, then one shall also never make it become green, and never red. <note>The author is describing the alchemical stages of transformation: Nigredo (blackness/stinking), Albedo (whiteness/winter), and eventually reaching the goal through the green and red stages.</note>

Yet in the juice of the grape, the air is draped in a different garment than in other fruits of corn or grains.

Here I say that the air is the life of all things, just as it is also coagulated in the Wine.

### or the Vinegar of the Sages.

<sig>A 4</sig>

I have seen a certain man at my house, *Mr. Snyders*, who, along with his wife, was dressed in very little cloth <note>original: *laken*; a type of expensive woolen fabric. To be "dressed in little cloth" suggests they were poor or humble in appearance.</note>, so that at that time I did not think much of him: also, I had little opportunity then to speak with him, since some friends had come to visit me at my house, for which reason *Snyders* took his leave of me, and afterward I thought no more of him. But 12 years later, he came again to the house where I still live. This was early one morning; and as he greeted me, and I him in return, I looked at him and he became known to me; whereby I asked him if he were not *Mr. Snyders*? and he said: "yes, I am the man himself." 

Upon this, he showed me a ring, and said that I should test what it was. # The Golden Lion,

<term>mercury</term> <note>symbol: ☿; also known as quicksilver, considered the "mother" of all metals in alchemy.</note> is master over <term>gold</term> <note>symbol: ☉; the perfect metal, associated with the Sun.</note> and other metals. When I saw that my mercury was consumed, and that the gold remained as it was before, I spoke to *Snyders*: "What devil of a metal is that, which consumes the mercury?" He said, "do your best to make it fine." <note>In metallurgy, to make a metal "fine" means to purify or refine it to its highest quality.</note> So I took <term>sal ammoniac</term> <note>symbol: 🜔; ammonium chloride, used as a flux in refining.</note> and <term>sulfur</term> <note>symbol: 🜍; the active, combustible principle in alchemy.</note> together and applied them to the gold, and in that way, with great effort, I made it fine; and it was the most beautiful gold in the world, such that I have never held better in my hand. 

When our work was finished, he asked if we might share a drink together? He said, "I shall come early tomorrow"; as he indeed did, for he came knocking before the door was even open, and I let him in.

# ...on the Vinegar of the Sages.

...because minerals possess nothing more than earth, and their nature can produce no fruits as long as they are not mixed with the <term>▽</term> <note>The alchemical symbol for Water.</note> which is brought forth out of nature, which might perhaps be like the Snow, or the Salt of *Basilius* <note>Referring to Basil Valentine, a legendary 15th-century alchemist whose writings were highly influential in the 1600s.</note>, which I have also often noted in the *Tartarum* <note>Original: "Tartarum." Tartar is the potassium bitartrate crust found in wine barrels, used in alchemy as a source of "fixed salt."</note> that comes forth from the old <term>♄</term> <note>The alchemical symbol for Lead, also representing the planet Saturn.</note>; and this is the true <term>▽</term> <note>Water.</note>, about which so much has been written.

The common earth in the beginning was brought forth from the water, just as one reads at the beginning of the book of *Genesis*; which earth can give no fruit without water: for water is her mother, who carried the earth in her womb, and still daily gives her breasts to the earth, which is the dew or the rain of Heaven. These come forth from the power of Heaven upon the earth, which is the Fountain of which Count *Bernardus* writes <note>Bernard Trevisan (1406–1490), a famous Italian alchemist. The "Fountain" refers to his famous allegory of the King’s bath, representing the process of dissolution.</note>. This Fountain is not the common Vinegar <note>Original: "Afijn," an archaic spelling of "azijn." In alchemy, "Vinegar" often refers to a powerful solvent rather than the household liquid.</note>, about which we must be warned; but it comes forth from the <term>☩</term> <note>The alchemical symbol for Antimony, here likely representing the "Earth" or the "primordial matter."</note>, and is its child; to which *Basilius*, writing in his first key <note>Referring to the "Twelve Keys of Basil Valentine," a foundational text of alchemical philosophy.</note>, also gives us much guidance.

# The Golden Lion.

->Metals and Minerals signify that upon which the entire art depends. For without the Salt, it is impossible to accomplish anything in this work.<-

Wine, when it is consumed by us, is transformed within the human body into blood; but when it is burdened with the cross <note>Original: "kruis." In alchemical literature, being "burdened with the cross" often refers to a substance being acidified, mortified, or put through a process of purification that makes it chemically "sharp" or potent, though often toxic to ingest.</note>, then it becomes a friend to Metals and Minerals, and a bad friend to mankind. I do not write this from hearsay, but because I know it to be true myself; for I have discovered this through the labor of my own hands, having obtained the *Anima* <note>Latin: "Soul." In alchemy, this refers to the internal "fire" or essential sulfur of a substance.</note> of the Minerals by means of the Wine. Yet the Wine of Metals is different from common wine, though it must nonetheless be produced by means of Wine and prepared with distilled Vinegar; a subject which I shall treat further in another place.

When one wishes to obtain the *Anima* of the Metals, one must first know the nature of the Metals from which the *Anima* is to be extracted. Even though all Metals originate from the same root—namely from <term>☉</term> <note>The symbol for the Sun, representing Gold.</note>, <term>♃</term> <note>The symbol for Jupiter, representing Tin.</note>, and <term>☿</term> <note>The symbol for Mercury, representing Quicksilver.</note>—nevertheless, each of the Metals requires its own specific treatment. But if one wishes to seek the Salt, about which so much has been written, then it must be produced through the Metals and with the Metals; and with <term>♄</term> <note>The symbol for Saturn, representing Lead.</note>, which with the cross <note>The text ends here; the catchword "cross" indicates the sentence continues on the next page, likely describing the "crucial" transformation of lead.</note>

### ...of the Vinegar of the Sages,

[that which] is burdened with the cross <note>Original: "kruis." As noted previously, being "burdened with the cross" refers to a substance being acidified or mortified through chemical transformation.</note> must be the beginning and the end; even though Basil Valentine <note>Original: "Basilius." Referring to the legendary 15th-century alchemist Basilius Valentinus.</note>, Paracelsus, and others write further about this, saying that one must seek it through imperfect Minerals.

What is then more imperfect than <term>Saturn</term> <note>The symbol ♄, representing Lead.</note>, and <term>Mars</term> <note>The symbol ♂, representing Iron.</note>, and <term>Mercury</term> <note>The symbol ☿, representing Quicksilver.</note>? These are placed by the Ancient Sages <note>Original: "Oude Wijfen." While literally "old women," in this context it refers to traditional practitioners or the "old wives' tales" of early chemical philosophy.</note> before <term>Gold</term> <note>The symbol ☉, representing the Sun or Gold.</note>, because they cost very little; yet they are indeed very good. But there is yet another <term>Gold</term>, which is called the *Green Lion*, and it is the ancient <term>Mercury</term> which one finds marked with a cross from its very birth. Therefore, learn to know nature through the beginning of the Metals.

And to have a small light concerning the nature of the Metals, and to know from what they were made in God’s first work; one cannot investigate this better than by burning them to dust and ash, so that nothing remains but a calx <note>Original: "kalk." In alchemy, a "calx" is the fine powder or "oxide" left after a metal has been roasted or "killed" by fire.</note>, or dead Metal. Then one shall find that in this calx a Salt remains: from which one can see clearly enough what the beginning of the Metals must be, namely, that they are of a salty nature: for in the end of a thing, one can observe what its origin was. When salt is melted into a water, one sees from this that its origin was water: and in this way all things were made by God through His Word out of water, air, and fire; just as one...

# ...of the Vinegar of the Sages.

The Ancient Philosophers presented time to us through parables, yet with clear words. When they speak of *Leo* <note>The Lion, representing the astrological sign or a specific stage of heat</note>, the common man thought of gold; whereas it is nothing other than the time, which they have called gold: and when they speak of *Mars*, they took that for the Salt, which is made or caught in the month of March <note>The word "Mars" and "March" share the same root; alchemists often sought "celestial salt" from morning dew in this month</note>, through the power of the Magnet-stone <note>Lodestone; used here metaphorically for an attractive force</note>. For when I had made the Salt from Iron <term>♂</term>, nothing appeared to me but a common Vitriol of Iron <term>♂</term> <note>Iron sulfate, often called "green vitriol"</note>, which is in all metals: for they all come from one and the same mother.

# ...of the Vinegar of the Sages.

...nothing can be done without the Metals: for from the Metals comes a key, with which one can lock up the Salt <note>In alchemy, to "lock up" or "fix" a salt means to make a volatile substance stable so it can withstand heat.</note>, without which it is impossible to expect any profit; of which I have written in my mining-booklet, *The Cabinet of Minerals*.

From tartar <term>wijnsteen</term> <note>A sediment found in wine barrels, used as a flux in metalworking and as a medicinal base.</note> one can make very beautiful Medicines; but I have not used it for that work; for which reason I shall not write of it more extensively, other than to say that it is a good flux-powder for very many Metals, and the most suitable stone for melting them. Hereby all friends of this art may know, that the Wine of the Sages is another drink: for the Philosophers say, that the wind has carried it in its belly <note>A reference to the *Emerald Tablet* of Hermes Trismegistus, a foundational alchemical text.</note>, and that it is in all places. After that they also say, that the tartar of the Sages is not the common tartar, but that which is born through Saturn <term>♄</term> <note>Saturn/Lead</note>. For this reason it is easy to see, that the Ancient Sages meant neither the common wine, nor the Saturn <term>♄</term>, and that one should not use them in such a raw state, but rather [they meant] its soul and the Salt, of which Count *Bernard* <note>Bernard of Trevisan, a 15th-century Italian alchemist famous for his allegorical "Fountain."</note> speaks in his Fountain; that it is beautiful and transparent like Crystal; and *Paracelsus* <note>The famous 16th-century Swiss-German physician and alchemist.</note> gives it the name of the *Secret of Tartar* <note>Original Latin: *Arcanum tartari*</note>, and this is nothing other than the old wine, being very clear, of which others have written more.

# The Golden Lion,

I say then once more, that it is impossible for anything to come forth without the time that God has ordained. Farmers are much better Philosophers than we are; for they let nature work through the seasons of the year. Therefore, let us observe the proper time and prune the vine, so that we may drink the wine that God grants us through His grace: for the Almighty works through nature to make known to us humans what powers reside within nature. 

We ought to follow nature, and then the Vitriol of the Sages <term>Vitriool der Wijsen</term> <note>Vitriol usually refers to sulfate minerals. In alchemy, the "Vitriol of the Sages" is a metaphorical or secret substance, not the common chemical version.</note> would not long remain hidden from us: for this is not the common vitriol made through distillation, of which *Glauber* <note>Johann Rudolf Glauber (1604–1670) was a famous Dutch-German chemist and alchemist, known for discovering "Glauber's salt."</note> writes in his book entitled *The Stone of the Philosophers* <note>original: "Lapis Philoſophorum"</note>; and therein *Glauber* teaches that common Oil of Vitriol <note>A historic name for concentrated sulfuric acid.</note> is the stone of the philosophers. 

If that stone were to be made from [common] vitriol, then many would have possessed it before *Glauber*, and before me as well: for never has any Philosopher named the true name of the matter <note>The "Prima Materia" or first matter needed to begin the alchemical Great Work.</note> from which the stone of the Sages must be made. What then would be in vitriol? Nothing other than medicine for man, but it can find no permanence <term>stant</term> <note>Meaning stability or "fixity"—the ability of a substance to remain stable under high heat without evaporating.</note> upon the Metals. For this reason, the friends of the art <note>The "art" refers to the practice of alchemy.</note> must be entreated by me as a brother,

### To Obtain the Soul of Venus.

Take 1 pound of Verdigris <note>original: *Spaens-groen*; a green pigment historically made by exposing copper to acetic acid vapors</note> that is made from <term>Copper (♀)</term> <note>represented by the symbol for Venus</note> by means of <term>Sal Ammoniac (⚹)</term> <note>ammonium chloride, a salt used as a powerful solvent in alchemical processes</note>; this shall be pounded small in a mortar, and then good <term>Sal Ammoniac (⚹)</term> poured upon it; and when it is all dissolved into a green <term>liquid (aqua)</term> <note>original: *aqua*; Latin for water or liquid</note>, then it shall be filtered <note>original: *gedistilleert*; here referring to 'distillatio per filtrum' or filtering through a cloth</note> through a felt paper until everything is entirely clear. This <term>Water (▽)</term> <note>the alchemical symbol for water or solution</note> must be boiled over a gentle <term>Fire (△)</term> <note>the alchemical symbol for fire</note> until it begins to become dry, and then one shall pour our <term>Sal Ammoniac (⚹)</term> upon it 3 or 4 times; thus the <term>Sal Ammoniac (⚹)</term> dissolves the metal, and then you obtain the red soul of the <term>Copper (♀)</term>. It is to be marveled at that such green juice can change into a blood-red, wonderful to behold; and when it has been thus boiled, one shall dissolve it with common <term>Water (▽)</term> and have it filtered through a felt paper, as was taught before.

# De Goude Leeuw,

...would give anything something that it does not have. Therefore, it is nothing other than lies and deceit to make the Noble *Alchemy* infamous, whereby divine nature is slandered and held to be false by the common man; of which more will be discussed hereafter.

### ->To Obtain the Soul of Venus.<-

Take: One pound of Verdigris <note>original: *Spaens-groen*; a green pigment or crust formed on copper</note> that is made from <term>Venus (♀)</term> <note>the alchemical symbol for copper</note> by means of <term>Sal ammoniac (⚹)</term> <note>ammonium chloride, a salt used as a powerful solvent in alchemical work</note>; this shall be pounded small in a mortar, and then good <term>Sal ammoniac (⚹)</term> poured upon it; and when it has all been dissolved into a green *water* <note>original: *aqua*</note>, then it shall be filtered <note>original: *gedistilleert*; specifically 'distillatio per filtrum', the process of clarifying liquid through a filter</note> through a felt paper until everything is entirely clear. This <term>Water (▽)</term> one must boil on a gentle <term>Fire (△)</term> until it begins to become dry, and then one shall pour our <term>Sal ammoniac (⚹)</term> upon it 3 or 4 times; thus the <term>Sal ammoniac (⚹)</term> dissolves the metal, and then you obtain the red soul of <term>Venus (♀)</term>, which is a thing of wonder: that such a green juice can change into a blood-red, wonderful to behold; and when it has been thus boiled, one shall dissolve it with common <term>Water (▽)</term> and have it filtered through a felt paper, just as was taught before.

To proceed further, I shall now come to the extraction of the *Soul* (Anima) of certain salts.

To prevent this, one can use no better means than a furnace with two chimneys, such as I have described in my *Cabinet of Minerals*; and if one works within it, the poisonous smoke shall not harm you.

### *How by means of <term>Antimony</term> <note>original symbol: ♁</note> Lead can be made so hard that it gives a ring like Tin; though it does not attain such brightness.*

<term>Take</term> <note>original: ℞, the traditional symbol for a recipe or prescription.</note> 4 pounds of <term>Antimony</term> <note>original symbol: ♁</note>, and one pound of old pieces of iron nails. These old nails must first be placed in the crucible or melting pot and made glowing hot before the <term>Antimony</term> <note>original symbol: ♁</note> is added to them; and the <term>Antimony</term> must be pounded small and fine before it is added to that iron. When the <term>Mars</term> <note>original symbol: ♂; the alchemical name for Iron.</note> is then glowing in the crucible, add the <term>Antimony</term> <note>original symbol: ♁</note> onto it, and let it stand for half an hour in the <term>Fire</term> <note>original symbol: △</note>, which must be driven strongly. After this, one must pour it out into a casting-pot, and so one shall find a <term>King</term> <note>original: "Konink"; this refers to the "regulus," the pure metallic mass that settles at the bottom of a crucible after smelting, separated from the waste.</note>; knock that then off from the Slag <note>The stony waste matter separated from metals during smelting.</note>. This shall be as bright as silver, and very hard and brittle; this is then the *King of Mars* <note>original: "Regulus Martis"; an alloy of iron and antimony often prized by alchemists for the star-like patterns that form on its surface upon cooling.</note>. If it is then not yet clean enough, one can put it back into the crucible and melt it once more: when it is now melted, throw a little <term>Salt</term> <note>original symbol: 🜔</note> into it, then the <term>King</term> will become very clean, to be used for your work.

As... <note>Catchword: "Als," indicating the first word of the following page.</note>

->*If you then wish to have the <term>Lead</term> <note>original symbol: ♄; Saturn</note> hard, then do thus.*<-

<term>Take</term> <note>original: ℞</note> 12 pounds of <term>Lead</term> <note>original symbol: ♄</note>, and one pound of the <term>King</term> <note>original: "Konink"; the "regulus" or pure metallic mass produced in the previous step.</note>; these melted together make the lead hard, and suitable to cast pipes for pumps from it. If one takes one pound of this King against 4 pounds of <term>Lead</term> <note>original symbol: ♄</note>, that makes it so hard that one might well cast cannons from it. If one also takes one pound of this King against 4 ½ pounds of <term>Lead</term> <note>original symbol: ♄</note>, then it gives a good ring <note>Referring to the acoustic resonance of the metal when struck.</note>; and when long bars are cast from it, the book-printers come to make their letters <note>Type metal used for printing presses.</note> from it.

This King can indeed be put into <term>Tin</term> <note>original symbol: ♃; Jupiter</note>, but it makes the same bluish, and it is then not as good as that which is made from <term>Copper</term> <note>original symbol: ♀; Venus</note> and <term>Iron</term> <note>original symbol: ♂; Mars</note>.

When *Glauber* <note>Johann Rudolf Glauber (1604–1670), a significant German-Dutch chemist and alchemist.</note> now writes that <term>Antimony</term> <note>original symbol: ♁; referring to stibnite or the metallic element</note> would be the Salt of the Wise, and also teaches in what manner it should be prepared, it makes me wonder why others before him did not also make the Philosopher's Stone from it. For if the oil of Vitriol <note>Concentrated sulfuric acid.</note> were the Salt of the Wise, then all the Distillers would possess the *Stone* <note>original: "Lapis," the Philosopher's Stone</note>: *Glauber* writes that the red oil from Vitriol would be the Red Lion’s Blood <note>A symbolic name for a powerful alchemical reagent, usually a deep red solvent or substance.</note>, which nevertheless was never made by him in his lifetime.

## or the Vinegar of the Sages.

*Gems*, in blood, in old rags, in the hair of beasts, in gold, in silver, in iron, in copper, in tin, in lead, in mercury, in sulfur, in arsenic, and furthermore in everything one can imagine, nothing has been found that might serve the Work. Although one must perform the preparation through the metals, the metals are nevertheless not the matter of the Philosopher's Stone, but only the body of the matter of the Sages: for the true matter of the Work, as far as I can judge, is the spirit of the world, which is coagulated in the earth and joined to the metals, to make one out of two. Concerning this, I have written here before that with <term>sal ammoniac</term> <note>original symbol: ⚹; ammonium chloride</note> the *Anima* <note>Latin for "soul"; refers to the internal essence or "sulfur" of a metal.</note> and its salts can be drawn out from all the metals. This is nothing other than a <term>Vitriol</term> <note>original symbol: 🜔; a sulfate mineral</note> of the metals, and yet far more glorious than common <term>vitriol</term>; however, this is still not the <term>Vitriol of the Sages</term>. And so that you may know what the Vitriol of the Sages is, I say that it must be a single thing that has not yet taken on a physical form; and this is the invisible air, which unites itself with all seeds of the whole world, wherever it can reach. Therefore, cease using the metals and salts to make your oil; for it is not in them.

## The Golden Lion,
<meta>catchword: for</meta>

ways extracted fine **Salt** through <term>☩</term> <note>original: "Antimony"; a brittle, silvery-gray metalloid often used in alchemical purification</note>, and made that into a sweet **Salt**; yet I have been able to achieve nothing with it. I also made an **Oil** from that **Salt**, but I can find no use for it anywhere. This **Oil** was as sweet as sugar; and when I let it cool in the pot in which I had made it, it was as transparent and clear as a **Ruby**.

->*Its preparation is in this manner.*<-

**℞** <note>original: "Recipe"; Take</note>. **White-lead**, or **Minium** <note>red lead</note>, or **Lead-ash**, or **Litharge** <note>lead monoxide</note>, and of one of these as much as you wish. This shall be dissolved in <term>🜚</term> <note>original: "Acetum"; Vinegar</note>, and then distilled through a felt-paper: the *feces* <note>original: "feces"; the solid dregs or residue remaining after filtration</note> that remain in the felt shall be cast away, and the distilled *aqua* <note>original: "aqua"; the liquid solution or "water"</note> shall be boiled in an earthen pot until it is dry; and then some more <term>🜚</term> poured upon it, and boiled again as before, until it is a thick, beautiful red **Oil**.

In this way one can also make a red **Oil** out of <term>♄</term> <note>original: "Saturn"; the alchemical name for Lead</note>, to be used in Medicine. In the Metals it shall also do its work well, if it is prepared in a proper manner; which shall be treated further hereafter; for each process also has its own particular way of working. Let this be a warning to you from me, as a Friend:

# ...or of the Vinegar of the Sages.

<sig>E 2</sig>
<meta>catchword: Work</meta>

for I have not copied it from anyone else, but have even worked it with my own hands, and discovered it at my own expense; for which I must thank Almighty God.

In *Libra* <note>original: "Libra"; the Balance. In alchemy, this often refers to the stage of sublimation or weighing proportions</note> it comes forth, through *Leo* <note>original: "Leo"; the Lion. Often symbolizing fixed sulfur or the heat of the furnace</note> it is fixed, until in *Pisces* <note>original: "Pisces"; the Fish</note>, or in the Waterman <note>Aquarius</note>; there it changes into *Aries* <note>original: "Aries"; the Ram</note>, or in the Capricorn, of which others have written enough.

When I first began to read these things, of *Libra*, *Leo*, *Aries*, *Pisces*, it seemed to me that these signs were placed there as vain decorations; but now that I have experienced the same for myself, I see that the **Philosophers** <note>a term for historical alchemists</note> have indicated the foundations of the **Art** <note>Alchemy</note> therewith. ### or the Vinegar of the Sages.

This putrefaction must occur through the Salt’s own spirit, without adding any foreign Salt to it, about which I intend to treat at another time. ### *The Preparation of this Butter from Antimony <term>Antimony</term> <note>original symbol: ♁</note> is as follows.*

℞ <note>Recipe or "Take"</note> One pound of Antimony <term>Antimony</term> <note>original symbol: ♁</note> and half a pound of Mercury Sublimate <term>Mercury Sublimate</term> <note>original symbol: ☿</note>; grind this together as finely as possible. Afterward, place it in a glass retort coated with lute <note>original: *leer*—a clay or paste used to seal and protect glass vessels from high heat</note>: then it shall be driven in a sand bath <term>sand bath</term> <note>original: *zand-kapelle*—a vessel of sand used to distribute heat evenly</note> according to common usage for 5 to 6 hours over common fire <term>fire</term> <note>original symbol: △</note>. In this way, first a white spirit comes over; afterward, the "butter" <note>Antimony trichloride, which has a thick, oily consistency like butter.</note> comes into the neck of the retort. At that point, the Mercury <term>Mercury</term> <note>original symbol: ☿</note> is still partly in the form of a red cinnabar <note>Mercury sulfide, a bright red mineral.</note>, which some call the Cinnabar of Antimony <note>original: *Sinnaber van den ♁*</note>, though without reason; for with the Mercury that was added to the Antimony and has separated from it through distillation, there is, to my knowledge, not much to be accomplished, neither in medicines nor in metals.

This distilled butter shall be taken, and as much Mercury <term>Mercury</term> <note>original symbol: ☿</note> added to it as the butter weighs, and then again as much Antimony <term>Antimony</term> <note>original symbol: ♁</note> as was taken at first. Distill that again as before, and then no more butter will come over, but a Vinegar of Antimony <note>original: *Asijn van den ♁*—likely referring to a more acidic, liquid distillate.</note>.

# or of the Vinegar of the Sages

Through the cross and sorrow the world is overcome: whoever can conquer the world through Fire <term>Fire</term> <note>original symbol: △; in alchemy, fire is the primary agent of transformation and purification</note>, I believe he is a Master and Friend of Nature. I would write more of this now, but I intend to discuss it again in another book, if God pleases. Yet I say, that this world is nothing other than the Old Saturn <term>Saturn</term> <note>original symbol: ♄; Saturn typically represents the metal Lead, but in a philosophical sense, it refers to the "prima materia" or the raw, chaotic state of matter</note>, a God of the Philosophical Sea, who is described by Sendivogius <note>Michael Sendivogius (1566–1636), a famous Polish alchemist known for his works on the "Central Nitre" and the "Philosophical Sea"</note>. He showed him Fire <term>Fire</term> <note>original symbol: △</note> under a rocky cliff, a mine of Gold <term>Gold</term> <note>original symbol: ☉</note>, and another of steel; with which I shall now let this rest.

But I must return again to the Salts and Minerals, without which the Old Saturn <term>Saturn</term> <note>original symbol: ♄</note> is not to be obtained; and even if one already had common Lead <term>Lead</term> <note>original symbol: ♄</note>, it still could find no standing in the Art <note>the "Art" refers to Alchemy or the "Great Work"</note>. # The Golden Lion,

### The Beginning of the Work.

The Furnace or Instruments in which one distills the Sulfur <term>Sulfur</term> <note>original: "Solfer"</note> are nothing other than an earthenware sugar-baker's mold <note>A conical ceramic mold used in sugar refining, repurposed here as a makeshift furnace body</note>, broken off slightly at the top; upon this, one places a glass helm <term>Helm</term> <note>the top part of a still, also known as an alembic head, which collects vapors</note>, and thus the furnace is ready for distilling. This Furnace is placed on an iron trivet <term>Trivet</term> <note>an iron stand or tripod used to hold vessels over a fire</note>, high enough from the ground that the small pan with the Antimony <term>Antimony/Sulfur</term> <note>original symbol: ♁; though technically the symbol for Antimony, the author uses it here for the sulfurous starting material</note> can fit under it, and neither higher nor lower; for if the Furnace stood too low, then the Fire <term>Fire</term> <note>original symbol: △</note> would be smothered, and if the Furnace were higher from the pan, then all the spirit <term>Spirit</term> <note>the volatile, gaseous essence of the substance being distilled</note> would escape outside the Furnace. When the Furnace is thus ready, then take the receiver <term>Receiver</term> <note>the vessel, usually glass, placed at the end of the still to collect the condensed liquid</note> which is large, and fill it half full with common rainwater <term>Water</term> <note>original symbol: ▽</note>, and lay it before the beak of the Helm, so that the beak or the pipe is about 2 fingers' breadth above the Water <term>Water</term> <note>original symbol: ▽</note> that is in the receiver. ***N.B.*** <note>Latin: *Nota Bene*, meaning "Note Well"</note> The receiver must not be luted <term>Luted</term> <note>original: "geluteert"; to be sealed with "lute," a paste used to make laboratory joints airtight</note>, otherwise the spirits would suffocate, and nothing of the spirit would come over.

When your Furnace or pot upon which the Helm stands, with its receiver, is thus prepared, then take your finely crushed Antimony <term>Antimony/Sulfur</term> <note>original symbol: ♁</note>, put it into the earthenware pan, and place it under the Furnace before you put the Fire <term>Fire</term> <note>original symbol: △</note> into it; and when it is thus ready, take a small splinter of dry wood, or a sulfur-match <note>original: "swavel-stok"</note>, with which you must [put] the fire therein...

When the Antimony <term>Antimony</term> <note>original symbol: ♁</note> is burning, one should continue to add fresh Antimony until one has enough Medicine, and then let the Antimony burn out. The receiver containing the Milk of Antimony <term>Milk of Antimony</term> <note>original: "Lac ♁"; a white, milky suspension or distillate of antimony, highly prized in iatrochemistry</note> must then be well-stoppered so that its powers do not evaporate; for the spirit is not stable, such that it is drawn up by the air. But if you come to unite its Salt <term>Salt</term> <note>the fixed, crystalline residue after calcination</note> with it, then the spirit has a magnet that holds and strengthens it, a matter which I intend to treat in another book.

This Medicine is a glorious thing for those who have developed any ailment of the lungs through heavy drinking or otherwise; and to cure this condition, one may use half a pint of it every morning, or as much as one sees fit.

In the ash that remains below, one has the Salt of Glory <term>Salt of Glory</term> <note>original: "Sout der heerlijkheit"; likely referring to the purified alkaline salt extracted from the calcined remains of the antimony</note>; the Oil is above in the Helm <term>Helm</term> <note>the top part of the still where vapors condense</note>; thus you have three things from one piece of Antimony, which shall suffice for this time. <insert>See the Red Lion, page 71.</insert>

To return now to the Metals and Minerals, I say further that the Antimony of the Wise <term>Antimony of the Wise</term> <note>original: "♁ der Wyſen"; the philosophical or "living" substance sought by alchemists, distinguished from common crude antimony</note> is not in these, but through these the Antimony of the Wise is made; yet I write this

And so that you might understand this, take an example from man, who is created and made after God’s image: in what manner his life is sustained with food and drink, and how the food is salted with many and various Salts; and so must our Metallic food also be.

# The Golden Lion,

<meta>catchword: there</meta>

I dissolved **Tin** <note>original symbol: ♃</note> and **Lead** <note>original symbol: ♄</note>—4 <term>lots</term> <note>A traditional unit of weight, usually between 14 and 17 grams</note> of Tin and 3 lots of Lead. These reacted so strongly and with such great force that they eventually became dry, turning into a <term>calx</term> <note>A powdery metallic oxide created through heating or "calcination"</note>. Into this calx I added 3 lots of **Antimony** <note>original symbol: ♁</note>; then the calx began to burn again and dissolved the Antimony once more. When this mass had been burned dry into a calx, I took 4 lots of **Mercury Sublimate** <note>original symbol: ☿; a corrosive compound of mercury and chlorine</note> and added it to the mixture, which caused it to burn even more intensely.

Afterward, I took this mixed material and placed it in a coated <term>retort</term> <note>A glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distilling chemicals</note>. Upon distilling it, a white spirit passed over into the <term>receiver</term> <note>The vessel used to collect the condensed vapors</note>, which constantly reacted or fermented; this is a **water** <note>original: "aqua"</note> about which much could be written. The <term>dead head</term> <note>original: "Caput Mortuum"; the solid residue left in the vessel after the volatile spirits have been distilled away</note> that remained in the retort is like a black <term>regulus</term> <note>A metallic mass or "button" found at the bottom of a crucible</note> of Lead, Tin, **Copper** <note>original symbol: ♀</note>, and Antimony, which must be dissolved again in this spirit.

*Note:* If one wishes to reduce this <term>dead head</term> to dust and ashes, one must combine the previous composition and add half as much **common salt** <note>original symbol: 🜔</note> to it, then distill the whole mixture together. By doing this, one obtains a true **metallic water** that can achieve many things, which I intend to discuss further hereafter. Yet believe me, I have written as much here as any man in the world has done; for this knowledge is a light within the **Salts of the Metals**. Now there is still another...

### ...or the Vinegar of the Sages,

...another way to obtain the Salts of Metals and Minerals, which Salt is the Matter itself; and that is found mostly in **Iron** <note>original symbol: ♂</note>, and in old **Lead** <note>original symbol: ♄</note>, but primarily in old Wine, and in common Salts, such as **Sea Salt** <note>original symbol: 🜔</note>, Saltpeter, Salt, **Vitriol** <note>original symbol: 🜪</note>, **Gold** <note>original symbol: ☉</note>, **Antimony** <note>original symbol: ♁</note>, **Sal Ammoniac** <note>original symbol: 🜹</note>, **Sulfur** <note>original symbol: 🜍</note>, **Water** <note>original symbol: 🜄</note>, **Earth** <note>original symbol: 🜃</note>, **Fire** <note>original symbol: 🜂</note>, **Air** <note>original symbol: ♒︎</note>, and **Mercury** <note>original symbol: ☿</note>; **Mercury** is everything in all things; yet not the common kind, but that which is produced through the Art from this above-mentioned Matter.

One breaks **Iron** <note>original symbol: ♂</note> in **Water** <note>original symbol: 🜄</note>: into this "Red Sea" <note>An alchemical metaphor for a corrosive or transformative red solvent</note> one adds **Mercury Sublimate** <note>original symbol: ☿; a highly toxic compound of mercury and chlorine</note>. From this, a sour mist is driven. First, there comes a white **Earth** <note>original symbol: 🜃</note>; afterward the mist. In the bottom of the glass or the **retort** <note>A glass vessel with a long neck used for distilling</note> you shall find the **dead head** <note>original: "Caput Mortuum"; the solid residue remaining after all volatile parts have been evaporated</note>, which is then dark red, or of a very beautiful purple color; **Basilius** <note>Basilius Valentinus, a famous, likely pseudonymous, 15th-century alchemist</note> has also written further about this purple color of **Mercury**.

I say then, according to my feeling, that the Salts are the principal Matter of the work in this Art: for there are three Salts which the Philosophers command us to use for this Art, namely Vegetable, Animal, and Mineral.

The Vegetable Salt is in all the fruits that grow above the earth; yet each Salt has its own fine and particular nature in the operation of the work, of which I shall treat more broadly hereafter, and therefore for this...

**Nitre** <term>⦶</term> <note>Also known as Saltpeter</note> is a master in itself. **Salt** <term>⊖</term> <note>Common sea salt or mineral salt</note> is a master in itself. **Vitriol** <term>🜔</term> <note>A metallic sulfate, often copper or iron-based</note> is a master in itself. These are the primary parts of Nature; therefore, accept this from me, all you Friends of this Art.

### of the Vinegar of the Sages.

When the dry Salts, namely the Nitre <term>①</term> <note>Saltpeter, a key ingredient in making strong mineral acids</note> and Alum <term>🜔</term> <note>An astringent mineral salt</note> are burnt out, and then dried again by common Fire <term>🜂</term>, and these Salts are mixed into the Mercury-water <note>original: "Aqua ☿"</note> that is made through Water <term>🜄</term>, then the Matter reacts so strongly that one cannot keep it in the glass, and it is yellowish in color. Through Gold <term>☉</term> and Nitre <term>①</term>, a Water <note>original: "Aqua"</note> or Alum-solution <term>🜔</term> is made, which dissolves the Gold very sweetly <note>without the violent corrosive action of strong acids like aqua regia</note> into a white nature; when one puts the Mercury <term>☿</term> therein, it falls down into an extraordinary powder, and the Water <note>original: "Aqua"</note> is clear, and the Gold floats like flakes in the Water <note>original: "Aqua"</note>, which it does not do with other Waters; yet this must stand for 3 to 4 days.

### or the Vinegar of the Sages.

<margin>G</margin>

...red **Water** <note>original: "Aqua"; in alchemy, an "aqua" is any liquid solvent or distilled spirit</note>, which is the fine **Soul** <note>original: "Anima"; the volatile essence or "life force" of a mineral</note>, and the **Sulfur** <term>🜍</term> <note>One of the Three Primes; it represents the principle of combustibility and the "soul" of the metal</note> of **Iron** [Mars] <term>♂</term>. This is bound, or gecoaguleert <note>coagulated; the process of turning a liquid into a solid or thick state</note>, by **Water** [Aqua] of **Sal Ammoniac** <term>🜔</term> <note>Ammonium chloride, a salt used to make metals more reactive</note> and **Mercury** <term>☿</term>, or **Water** [Aqua] of **Gold** <term>☉</term>, **Sal Ammoniac** <term>🜔</term>, and **Mercury** <term>☿</term>.

**Tin** [Jupiter] <term>♃</term> dissolved in **Sal Ammoniac** <term>🜔</term> gives a fiery lime <note>original: "kalk"; the powdery oxide or residue left after a substance has been heated or "calcined"</note> that binds itself; but if common **Mercury** <term>☿</term> is present, it yields a green **Water** [Aqua]: the remainder of the white lime falls to the bottom and into the **Green Sea** <note>The "Green Sea" or "Green Lion" is a common alchemical metaphor for a potent acidic solvent, typically a green-tinted vitriol or sulfuric acid solution</note>.

**Mercury** <term>☿</term> dissolved in **Sal Ammoniac** <term>🜔</term> makes the **Sal Ammoniac** <term>🜔</term> thick and unfit in its essence: for these are contrary things in Nature.

**Iron** [Mars] <term>♂</term> dissolved in **Sal Ammoniac** <term>🜔</term>, in which **Sal Ammoniac** <term>🜔</term> is present, makes the **Sal Ammoniac** <term>🜔</term> unfit: for these all strive against one another in Nature.

**Lead** [Saturn] <term>♄</term> dissolved in **Sal Ammoniac** <term>🜔</term> makes the **Water** [Aqua] sweet, if it remains standing upon it for a long time.

**Iron** [Mars] <term>♂</term> placed in **Oil of Sulfur** <term>🜍</term> <note>A concentrated acidic liquid made from sulfur</note> makes the oil sweet in taste, and these work together very gently. When these have been together for 6 to 7 days, one shall distill everything through a felt paper, and it will be a beautiful green: if this is boiled down over the **Fire** <term>🜂</term>, then one obtains beautiful square stones <note>This refers to the formation of crystals</note>. Therefore **Basilius** <note>Basilius Valentinus, a legendary 15th-century alchemist</note> speaks that **Iron** [Mars] <term>♂</term> protects **Copper** [Venus] <term>♀</term> with the sword, so that she would not become a harlot <note>An alchemical allegory: Iron (Mars) is used to purify Copper (Venus) and prevent it from reacting with unwanted elements</note>.

# The Golden Lion,

From Sal Ammoniac <term>🜔</term>, Gold <term>☉</term>, Sulfur <term>🜍</term>, Sal Ammoniac <term>🜔</term>, and Mercury <term>☿</term>, a Vinegar <term>☩</term> <note>original: "☩"; likely the Vinegar of the Sages, a specialized alchemical solvent</note> is made, which is very pleasant in taste; this contains a white Mercury <term>☿</term> within it, with which much could be accomplished, if only one knew its proper use.

From Gold <term>☉</term>, Sal Ammoniac <term>🜔</term>, Sal Ammoniac <term>🜔</term>, Salt <term>🜊</term>, Sal Ammoniac <term>🜔</term>, and Mercury <term>☿</term>, a Vinegar is also made, which carries a white Mercury <term>☿</term> with it. At the bottom of the retort <note>a glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distilling substances</note> remains the **Dead Head** <note>original: "Caput Mortuum"; the solid, often useless residue remaining after distillation or sublimation</note>; in this residue is the Salt, which one can leach out with common Water <term>🜄</term>, and then use alongside the spirit of this Mercury <term>☿</term>.

Lead <term>♄</term>, or Litharge <note>original: "Litargium"; a natural mineral form of lead(II) oxide</note> dissolved in Vinegar <term>☩</term>, then boiled down, yields a blood-red Oil. When this Oil is then dissolved with Water <term>🜄</term>, it makes the Water <term>🜄</term> sweet, and it draws the blood-red soul <note>original: "ziel"</note> toward itself. At the bottom is then the Salt, very beautiful and white. To this Oil of Lead <term>♄</term>, one may certainly add a blood-red **Soul** <note>original: "Anima"; the vital or volatile essence extracted from a metal</note> of Iron <term>♂</term>, and it will not ruin its color or brilliance.

Sal Ammoniac <term>🜔</term> burned through charcoal produces a green stone: when this is pulverized and dissolved by the air <note>a process called deliquescence, where a substance absorbs enough atmospheric moisture to turn into a liquid</note>, and then distilled through felt paper, a fiery **Water** <note>original: "Aqua"</note> is produced. This same liquid can be made sweet by a lye of common lime, which through distillation is driven into a white and then a red spirit <note>original: "geeft"; likely referring to the resulting vapors or "gifts" of the distillation process</note>. # The Golden Lion,

*Now I wish to turn myself toward the Salt Mountains <note>a common alchemical metaphor for the crystalline mineral foundations of the Great Work</note> that are situated between the East and the South, and therein is the glorious City of Hermes <note>referring to Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary father of alchemy; his "city" represents the heart of hermetic knowledge</note> endowed with three beautiful rivers, of which one is grey, the other red, and the third white. These come out of the East, extending toward the South, and they have their exit toward the West; these are the three Waters of our Sea: Saturn ♄ <note>traditionally associated with lead</note>, Mercury ☿ <note>quicksilver</note>, and Jupiter ♃ <note>traditionally associated with tin</note>.*

[magnificent] <note>This completes the word from the previous page, likely "heerlijk" (magnificent/glorious).</note>, which I bring forth to them: for the Princes rejoice with my milk, which comes from me; and the Kings of the earth are fattened by it. ...nothing. My entry <note>Referring to the consumption of wine.</note> is indeed sweet to people, but when I am used to excess, I bite like a serpent; indeed, I am an evil and venomous beast. ->## ...or the Vinegar of the Sages.<-

Philosophers! I am the serpent of the earth, and a wild, stinking dragon. I am also the Sun and the Moon of the World; the Salt that Nature has carried in her belly; a bird of the midnight, which is before every man’s eyes, but is not recognized. I am compared to Saturn <note>In alchemy, Saturn represents the metal lead and the "Black Phase" (*nigredo*), signifying the heavy, raw, or decayed state of matter before it is purified.</note>; yet I am no more than a common piece of wood when I am mistreated by those who do not know me. 

I am the blood of men, and the blood of the earth. I am the Central and Astral fire <note>"Central fire" refers to the heat within the earth that matures minerals, while "Astral fire" refers to the celestial influence or energy believed to descend from the stars into matter.</note>; and from the same source from which Gold, Silver, Copper, Tin, and Lead are made, I have also come forth. Whoever can dissect <note>Or "analyze/resolve" in a chemical sense.</note> me shall find that I am the first and the last among the Philosophers; and without me, no true Medicine <note>The "Universal Medicine" or Elixir, believed to cure all diseases and perfect metals.</note> for mankind can be made. 

So, to conclude my speech, I say further that those who are not satisfied with me may freely investigate Vitriol, Saltpeter, Common Salt <note>original: *Sal Commun*</note>, and all other salts besides, and they shall find what is right or wrong here.

# The Golden Lion,
H
<meta>catchword: because</meta>

break glasses in pieces, nor need to build many ovens, to thus smother your money in them: yet every baker bakes his bread in his own way. I believe that one could make earth out of <term>Fire</term> <note>original: △, the alchemical symbol for the element of fire</note>, out of earth a Salt, out of this Salt an Oil, out of that Oil a spirit, out of this spirit a body; but what the truth of this may be, is also still unknown to me. I write this as a dream; nevertheless I say, that from a blood-red Oil a white spirit can be driven through our distillation.

Everything I have written in this book, I give for nothing, which other sellers of the Art <note>original: "Konstverkoopers"; the author refers to "mercenary" alchemists who sold fraudulent recipes or charged high fees for secrets</note> would not do even for money: therefore I tell you still that you should not dishonor the Wine, so it shall also not dishonor you. I consider <term>Mercury</term> <note>original: ☿, the alchemical symbol for Mercury</note> to be the round World, in which there is a labyrinth without end, unless Lady Venus <note>In alchemy, Venus often represents copper or a specific "green" stage, but here the author explicitly identifies her help as the "Wine" and its derivatives</note> comes to offer him help, which is the Wine with all that proceeds from it. This Art is kept hidden by many to the honor of God, but indeed to the scorn and shame of Nature; yet he who slanders Nature, slanders God, for He has made Nature: do not mock Nature, and so you shall not be mocked by God; for God works through Nature, which He uses as a handmaid here below on earth, so that we may be helped by it.

<summary>The author warns against the financial ruin of traditional alchemy, claiming that his wine-based secrets are shared freely. He concludes with a philosophical defense of Nature as God's instrument, which should be studied with respect rather than hidden or mocked.</summary>

# ...of the Vinegar of the Sages.
H 4
<meta>catchword: because</meta> <note>original: "soo", connecting to the first word of the main text</note>

...if we wish to observe the times well. Now, because there are many who believe that not much depends upon the timing, such people must surely all go astray: for the time is as significant as the <term>Matter</term> <note>The physical substance used as the starting point for the alchemical work</note>, which is brought forth by time, and must be grasped by us at the [correct] time of year in order to bring the same back to its first essence. Therefore it is that I wish to have asked all the Friends of this <term>Art</term> <note>original: "Konst"; the standard term for the practice of alchemy</note> to indeed observe the time well, namely when Nature is open in her greenness, which I have called the <term>Vitriol</term> <note>Vitriol usually refers to metallic sulfates, but in this context, it refers to the "living" green energy or "Green Lion" of spring</note>; and this is nothing other than the greenness of the earth, which is mostly hidden in the Wine, since it contains all the powers of the Earth within itself, through which everything is brought forth.

After one takes up the Wine, it then receives various names. Frequently it is called a virgin, a gum, a honey, a milk, a <term>Vinegar</term> <note>original: "Afijn", an archaic form of "azijn"</note>, a <term>Tartar</term>, and also a "stone of stumbling" <note>A biblical reference to a "stumbling block" (1 Peter 2:8); here implying that the substance causes the prideful or ignorant to fail</note> for those who murder it <note>Likely referring to over-distillation or aggressive chemical processing that destroys the "vital spirit" of the substance</note> too much, over whom it shall rule, though they regard it so lightly; just as the Wine has spoken enough of itself in this book.

...made sweet. Thus much for this time.

The Wine is a drink of the Metals; if it stands upon them for some time, the Wine takes on the Nature of the Metals; likewise, whatever Salts it encounters, it takes on their Nature: the ⦶ <term>Antimony</term> <note>original: "Antimonium"</note> does exactly the same; when it has been calcined <note>burned to a powder to remove volatile parts</note>, it dissolves in the air. This *water* <note>original: "Aqua"</note> takes on the ♁ <term>Antimony</term> <note>original: "Stibium"</note> of the Metals, and it dissolves into a blood-red color: for all Salts calcined by the △ <term>fire</term> <note>original: "Vuur"</note> are Magnets <note>substances that attract and hold specific spirits or qualities</note>; but enough of this for now.

All Philosophers have also called distilling "calcining," because both can be done in the same manner in a single vessel, as I have described in the preceding sections.

The ♀ <term>Copper</term> <note>original: "Venus"</note> has four types of preparation or calcination: the first is in the open △ <term>fire</term>; the second, in a closed vessel; the third, by reverberation <note>a method where the flame or heat is reflected back onto the substance</note> in the flaming fire; the fourth, on a table above a gentle <note>original: "soet"; literally "sweet"</note> fire. There are two and two in this work that by Nature conflict with one another: namely, those that are calcined by the flaming △ <term>fire</term>, and those in the closed vessel, which I intend to discuss further hereafter.

The ⊖ <term>Alum</term> <note>original: "Aluin" or "Sal"</note> also has this same quality, when one half has been calcined by the △ <term>fire</term>, and the other half has had a spirit driven out of it.

It is the same with ☉ <term>Gold</term>, ☽ <term>Silver</term>, ♃ <term>Tin</term>, and ♂ <term>Iron</term>, which will be treated further hereafter.

Here, in brief, enough has been taught regarding the manner in which two conflicting *Bodies* <note>original: "Corpora"</note> can be brought out of one house <note>a single source material</note> and prepared.

I would not have described this here, had I not been driven to it because I recently—thinking I was dealing with a Lover of this Art—found that he understood as much of it as a beast that carries sacks to the mill. Such people still dare to call themselves Alchemists <note>original: "Alchymisten"</note>, and yet they in no way know what they have in their hands daily; such as the Oil of Vitriol <note>concentrated sulfuric acid</note>, the strong ▽ <term>water</term> <note>original: "▽"; Aqua Fortis or nitric acid</note>, and the Salts ① <term>Nitre</term> <note>saltpeter</note>, ⊖ <term>Salt</term>, ☿ <term>Mercury</term>, ♁ <term>Antimony</term>, ☩ <term>Antimony</term>, ⊝ <term>Alum</term>.

⚹ <term>Sal Ammoniac</term> <note>original: "⚹"; a volatile salt used in alchemical cooling and sublimation</note>, ☉ <term>Gold</term> <note>original: "☉"</note>, which nonetheless have a twofold spirit in one *Body* <note>original: "Corpus"</note>. There are some people who say, if they knew a man who had the Great Work <note>the *Magnum Opus*, the process for creating the Philosopher's Stone</note>, they would gladly give two or three tons of gold for it; and yet I believe they would not want to spend even a hundred, or even fifty Guilders <note>Dutch currency of the period</note> on it: nevertheless, I do not blame those people, because so many have been deceived. But with the true Artists <note>skilled practitioners of the art</note>, it happens in the very same way as one reads in *The Little Peasant* <note>original: "den kleinen Boer"; likely a popular fable or alchemical parable about a humble person who possesses great secret knowledge</note>; that his credit was lost among all his friends: just as Count *Bernard* <note>Bernard of Trevisan (1406–1490), a famous alchemist who famously spent his life and fortune in search of the Stone</note> also writes of the same, along with many other faithful Philosophers. He who wishes to know more of this may investigate it himself, as I have done; and they shall

# The Golden Lion,

...will find in truth what kind of adventure and treatment such people have encountered. <note>The text completes a thought from the previous page regarding the story of "The Little Peasant," a folk tale used here as an alchemical allegory.</note> It is with some people just as one sees with flies, which sit sucking on a stick until they have drawn the syrup or honey from it, and afterwards they befoul the place that provided them such sweet nourishment: such is also their ignorance and ingratitude; they would very much like to be wise, rich, and happy, but are unwilling to employ any means, nor go to any trouble or expense to obtain it all; and if someone should wish to show them the way, they would expect him to take all the burdens upon himself and bring the benefits easily to their door; for which they would then likely reward him with ingratitude and slander.

### On the Nature of Metals regarding the <term>Water</term> <note>represented by the symbol $\nabla$</note>, or <term>Strong-Waters</term>. <note>original: "Sterk-Waters"; historically, this refers to corrosive acids like nitric acid used to dissolve metals.</note>

# The Golden Lion,

When <term>Mars</term> <note>Iron, symbol: ♂</note> and <term>Jupiter</term> <note>Tin, symbol: ♃</note> are mostly present in there, they possess a <term>Magnet</term> <note>An alchemical "magnet" refers to an attractive force or a substance that "draws" others to it.</note>, and the <term>Mercury</term> <note>Quicksilver, symbol: ☿</note> attaches itself to it; this is a matter to be noted: it remains almost entirely intact and does not dissipate in the <term>solvent</term> <note>original symbol: 🜔, representing Water or Aqua Fortis (Nitric Acid)</note>, which is a marvel for those who cannot yet understand the nature of <term>Metals</term>; for Mars [Iron], <term>Venus</term> <note>Copper, symbol: ♀</note>, and Jupiter [Tin] are all three warm by nature, and <term>Saturn</term> <note>Lead, symbol: ♄</note> is cold. Therefore, the other metals push it away from themselves: for Saturn [Lead] is a cold Mercury, so that its "body" cannot easily be warmed. This is why other <term>Philosophers</term> <note>original: "Philosophen"; historically referring to alchemical authorities</note> also testify to this same cold body of Saturn—that it is the coldest among all the metals, such that none colder can be found, alongside <term>Antimony</term> <note>original symbol: 🜚</note>, because its smoke causes everything it touches to die; therefore, one must certainly avoid Saturn.

When one dissolves equal amounts of Jupiter [Tin] and Saturn [Lead] in the <term>solvent</term> <note>symbol: 🜔</note>, then it all turns into a milk, but it is still sour on the tongue. There must be a little less Saturn than Jupiter in it. When these two have then become an <term>Aqua</term> <note>original: "Aqua"; Latin for water, here meaning a liquid solution</note>, then take Mars [Iron] and put it in; then they shall react with one another again and become as red as blood. In this way, the <term>Planets</term> <note>The seven metals were traditionally linked to the seven planets; here it refers to the metallic solutions.</note> come to a standstill; but it still has a foul taste. This is then <term>killed</term> <note>original: "gedoot"; in alchemy, to "kill" a substance often means to fix it, neutralize its acidity, or change its reactive state.</note> by <term>calcined</term> <term>Antimony</term> <note>original symbol: ♁; "calcined" means reduced to a powder through high heat.</note>, and so that foul taste goes away. This calcined Antimony must melt in the air, in order to have its "life" from the air, and that

# ...of the Vinegar of the Wise.

...the better to wash away all corrosiveness; then you obtain a separation of its body, so that you may afterwards extract its Salt and <term>Alum</term> <note>original symbol: 🜔; here referring to a mineral salt or vitriol used in the process</note>, which must be produced by the Vinegar and the <term>Spirit of Wine</term> <note>distilled alcohol or ethanol</note>, which I shall discuss further hereafter.

<term>Saturn</term> <note>Lead, symbol: ♄</note> placed alone upon a <term>fire</term> <note>original: "tri"; referring to heat or a tripod furnace</note> makes the *Water* <note>original: "Aqua"; the solvent or acid</note> sweet and causes it to lose its fine sharpness. When this is <term>neutralized</term> <note>original: "gedoot" (killed); an alchemical term for fixing a substance or removing its volatile/corrosive qualities</note> with *Water of* <term>Sal Ammoniac</term> <note>original symbol: 🜛; ammonium chloride solution</note>, everything becomes a gum, or like thick milk. One can extract its Salt through the <term>Salmiac</term> <note>Ammonium chloride, symbol: 🜹</note>, and that also has fine virtues in the Art <note>the "Art" refers to Alchemy</note>.

If one pours that off into another glass, and then places <term>Mars</term> <note>Iron, symbol: ♂</note> into it, it becomes blood-red. This *Water* cannot become sweet upon Jupiter and Saturn, for the reason that there is more Jupiter in it than Saturn: for Jupiter is hot by nature, and therefore it cannot "kill" the heat, since they are both of <term>Fire</term> <note>original symbol: 🜂</note>, and being thus of one Nature, they cannot subdue one another; but if Saturn is the master there <note>meaning if Lead is the dominant substance in the mixture</note>, then the *Water* is overcome, becoming sweet and green; but with Jupiter it becomes thick and remains just as fiery on the tongue, which is not to be despised: for that

# The Golden Lion,

...that which is fiery has its life, which can be extracted by <term>Sal Ammoniac</term> <note>original symbol: ⚹; ammonium chloride</note>; regarding which I do not need to write much, for to anyone who has blackened their hands a little in the coals <note>a phrase meaning those who have practical experience in a laboratory or forge</note>, it is sufficiently well-known.

By leaching calcined <term>Antimony</term> <note>original symbol: ♁</note> with <term>Vitriol</term> <note>original symbol: ☩; a metallic sulfate used as a solvent</note>, one has prepared the Vitriol, and it is sweet of scent and brownish-red; but when one adds common <term>Water</term> <note>original symbol: 🜄</note> to it, it becomes white as milk, and this *Water* <note>original: "Aqua"</note> precipitates the *Soul* <note>original: "Anima"; the concentrated essence or spirit of a substance</note> of <term>Lead</term> <note>original symbol: ♄; Saturn</note>.

One makes a water from <term>Gold</term>, <term>Silver</term>, and <term>Copper</term> <note>original symbols: ☉, ☽, ♀</note> through calcination <note>the process of heating a metal until it oxidizes into a powder</note>, and then when one allows that calcined material to melt by itself in a damp cellar upon a glass table, one then obtains a very clear water that binds all dissolved metals, takes away their corrosiveness, and causes them to become sweet.

The <term>Mercury</term> <note>original symbol: ☿</note> dissolved in <term>Earth</term> <note>original symbol: 🜃; likely referring to a specific mineral base or "terra"</note> is green in color; the <term>Water</term> <note>original symbol: 🜄</note>, if one adds *Water of* <term>Salt</term> <note>original symbol: 🜔; likely brine or a spirit of salt</note> into it, becomes thick and yellow, as if it were precipitated with Oil of <term>Sulfur</term> <note>original symbol: 🜍</note>. This also has fine virtues in this Art. One can separate the Salt from the <term>Sal Ammoniac</term> <note>original symbol: ⚹</note> using <term>Vitriol</term> <note>original symbol: ☩</note>, which I have also discussed in the preceding sections.

An <term>Alkahest</term> <note>a hypothetical universal solvent sought by alchemists</note> turns the Vitriol into a blood-red oil. Common <term>Vitriol</term> <note>original symbol: ☩</note> also does this; yet not common <term>Alum</term> <note>original symbol: ⦵</note>, but rather that which has "fought" with <term>Iron</term> <note>original symbol: ♂; Mars</note>, of which *Basilius* <note>Basilius Valentinus, a semi-legendary 15th-century alchemist and monk known for his writings on antimony</note> speaks.

### ...of the Vinegar of the Sages.

...speaks, that <term>Mars</term> <note>original symbol: ♂; representing Iron</note> with his sword guards <term>Venus</term> <note>original symbol: ♀; representing Copper</note>, so that she should not become a harlot <note>In alchemy, a "harlot" often refers to a metal that reacts too easily or becomes "corrupted" by impurities; Mars/Iron is used here to stabilize or protect the Copper</note>; of which I shall treat more broadly in the following.

The <term>Sal Ammoniac</term> <note>original symbol: ※; ammonium chloride</note> becomes sweet when it stands upon an <term>Alkahest</term> <note>a theoretical universal solvent</note> for a long time; so that it does not need to be placed upon common <term>Lead</term> <note>original symbol: ♄; Saturn</note> when one wishes to have it sweet: for when it is sweet, then one draws the <term>Antimony</term> <note>original symbol: ☩; stibnite</note> off from it through common distillation, and then one has the Salt of the <term>Antimony</term>.

The <term>Spirit of Wine</term> <note>original: "Wijns geeft"; concentrated alcohol or ethanol</note>, placed upon calcined <term>Copper</term> <note>original symbol: ♀; Venus</note>, loses its <term>fire</term> <note>original symbol: 🜂</note> and becomes sweet in taste. This also has its virtues in the Art, for extracting the <term>Soul</term> <note>original: "Anima"; the essential spirit or quintessence of a substance</note> and the <term>Sulfur</term> <note>original symbol: 🜍</note> of metals and minerals.

One calcines <term>Antimony</term> <note>original symbol: ♁; also called "Spiesglas"</note> in various ways: over an open <term>fire</term> <note>original symbol: 🜂</note>, and also in a vessel or pot, as well as in a pan over coals, and also burnt out in a sweet fire; yet everyone may make it according to how they have need of it.

One calcines <term>Saltpeter</term> <note>original symbol: ①; potassium nitrate</note> using charcoal dust, or through <term>Copper</term> <note>original symbol: ♀</note> and <term>Saltpeter</term>—using an equal amount of each—also through <term>Antimony</term> <note>original symbol: ♁</note>, and then further through <term>Tin</term> <note>original symbol: ♃; Jupiter</note> and <term>Mercury</term> <note>original symbol: ☿</note>. One can also do the same with a <term>Regulus</term> <note>a metallic mass reduced from an ore</note> of <term>Iron</term> <note>original symbol: ♂; Mars</note> that has been made through <term>Antimony</term>. When the <term>Saltpeter</term> has been "killed" <note>neutralized or fixed so it no longer reacts violently</note> by this, and has then been kept at a glow for 12 hours, then one may take it out, and it shall be a gray mass.

### ->*A Medicine from <term>Antimony</term> <note>original symbol: ♁</note> for many ailments.*<-

### How one shall make a Spirit of Salt.

One <term>calcines</term> <note>heats a substance to high temperatures to drive off volatile parts or reduce it to powder</note> a pound of <term>common salt</term> <note>original: *Sout Commun*; sodium chloride</note> over an open <term>fire</term> <note>original symbol: △</note> in a pot until it is dry; once it is calcined, one must then dissolve it in three and a half pounds of rain <term>water</term> <note>original symbol: ▽</note>. When everything is dissolved, one shall add a <term>balance</term> <note>original symbol: ♎; likely indicating a specific weight or proportion</note> of <term>antimony</term> <note>original symbol: ♁</note> to it, and then distill it through a flask with a fine <term>alembic head</term> <note>original: *helm*; the cap of a still used to collect vapors</note> according to common practice. In this way, one first obtains a little <term>phlegm</term> <note>original: *flegma*; the watery, less potent first part of a distillation</note>, after which comes the <term>spirit</term> <note>original: *geest*; the concentrated acidic vapor</note>, which is very strong. This must be driven until all the spirit comes over, which is much stronger at the end than at the beginning. At the bottom there remains the <term>fixed salt</term> <note>original: *Sal fixum*; the non-volatile residue</note>, which melts very easily in the <term>fire</term> <note>original symbol: △</note> and in <term>water</term> <note>original symbol: ▽</note>, and it has a taste almost like <term>mercury</term> <note>original symbol: ☿; quicksilver</note>, but somewhat more pleasant. This is good for washing the gums of those who are tormented by <term>scurvy</term> <note>original: *scheurbuik*; a disease caused by Vitamin C deficiency, leading to bleeding gums and loose teeth</note> in the mouth and whose teeth stand loose: when they wash or rub them with this salt, they become firm again, and the stench then leaves the mouth. The spirit that has been distilled over is of great utility against scurvy in all the limbs, when one pours it into some wine, water, or any liquid, to the amount of 2, 3, 4, or 5 drops, depending on how strong one wants it or can tolerate it; and this, being drunk daily, heals those ailments in a person where other-

->*To make pure grain spirits.*<-

...distillation is made sweet and clarified, then it is prepared for the aforementioned use.

[...mountains] grow, or are produced from a spirit which the Philosophers call <term>Mercury</term> ☿ <note>Mercury/Quicksilver</note>, yet each has its own particular Nature, as one sees in <term>Lead</term> ♄ <note>Lead/Saturn; Lead was traditionally associated with the slow-moving planet Saturn</note> and <term>Tin</term> ♃ <note>Tin/Jupiter</note>, which sometimes lie in the same vein <note>A "vein" refers to a mineral deposit in the earth</note>, and yet they are of two different kinds when melting upon or in the <term>Fire</term> △ <note>Fire</note>.
