# of the Vinegar of the Wise.

to help. And so that you may be certain of its fine cold nature, take these two metals together, namely **Tin** <term>♃</term> <note>Jupiter</note> and **Lead** <term>♄</term> <note>Saturn</note>. Put these then into a **Water** <term>▽</term> <note>often referring to an acid or solvent in this context</note>, and you shall find that **Lead** <term>♄</term>, through its fine coldness, shall prevent the **Tin** <term>♃</term> from being dissolved by the **Water** <term>▽</term>: and if there is more **Tin** <term>♃</term> in it, then the **Water** <term>▽</term> will act upon the **Tin** <term>♃</term>, for as long as one can see, and leave the **Lead** <term>♄</term> lying there: for cold is opposed to heat. Therefore it is said, like seeks its like; namely, **Tin** <term>♃</term> seeks the **Sulfur** <term>🜍</term>; so also the Salts, such as **Salt** <term>🜔</term> and **Alum** <term>🜕</term> <note>original: "den 🜕", likely referring to Alum or Sal Ammoniac</note>. From these a **Water** <term>▽</term> is made, which is nothing other than the **Sulfur** <term>🜍</term> of the Salts; and the **Tin** <term>♃</term> is in its heart nothing else but a **Sulfur** <term>🜍</term> and a **Mercury** <term>☿</term>: yet this **Mercury** <term>☿</term> is somewhat more fixed <note>stable or less volatile</note> than common **Mercury** <term>☿</term>; and its **Sulfur** <term>🜍</term> is not so fixed as gold or silver; which one can discover when one makes *Painters' Gold* <note>A pigment made from tin, also known as mosaic gold, used to simulate gilding</note> from that **Tin** <term>♃</term>, as I have taught in the preceding [pages]. Through this I have also proven that it is a little Sun <note>Gold</note>, and **Lead** <term>♄</term> is a little Moon <note>Silver</note>, and **Mercury** <term>☿</term> is the *Aqua* <note>Water</note>. Understand well what is presented to you here.

**Tin** <term>♃</term> and **Lead** <term>♄</term> are contrary to one another, which is found when one melts them together. These shall turn to ash together, and this happens because the water and fire struggle against each other; if there is then too much **Water** <term>▽</term>, then that has the upper hand; and if there is too much **Fire** <term>△</term>, then that has the upper hand;

->The Golden Lion,<-

hand; that is, if there is more **Lead** <term>♄</term> present, then the **Tin** <term>♃</term> must perish; and if there is more **Tin** <term>♃</term>, then **Lead** <term>♄</term> must submit; and thus it goes with all the other metals.

When one places **Iron** <term>♂</term> and **Copper** <term>♀</term> together in a **solvent** <term>▽</term> <note>alchemical symbol for water or a liquid solvent</note>, the **solvent** <term>▽</term> will first dissolve the **Copper** <term>♀</term> and leave the **Iron** <term>♂</term> lying there. Therefore one need not think that **Lead** <term>♄</term> has a different sign than **Silver** <term>☽</term>; for they are by nature in friendship with one another, namely in the sign of **Aquarius** <note>the Water-Bearer</note>.

**Lead** <term>♄</term> has taken its body from **Mercury** <term>☿</term>, which is the first and the last in Nature; therefore one may well consider **Lead** <term>♄</term> as a "half-water" of the metals. This can also be read in **Paracelsus** <note>a famous 16th-century Swiss physician and alchemist</note> in his fine Seventh Rule of Metals, where **Lead** <term>♄</term> speaks of itself thus: *The stone of cold is in me, and that is also my water.*

# The Golden Lion,
<meta>catchword: Well</meta>

Among the animals I am also in their nature; but in the metals I am most often seen: for there is not one, even if it were gold itself, in which my form cannot be found. But whoever wants to have me must take me in the green time <note>A metaphor for the early, vital stage of the alchemical matter</note>, when I am still young, and my limbs are not shortened. When my wings <note>Symbolizing volatility or the ability to evaporate</note> are cut off by contrary things, I am of no virtue. <term>Saturn</term> <note>original: ♄; the metal Lead</note> and <term>Jupiter</term> <note>original: ♃; the metal Tin</note> find their joy with me, especially with my spirit, when they are embraced by me; and then we create the leaf of the metals and minerals. Therefore, hold <term>Mercury</term> <note>original: ☿; Quicksilver</note> in honor, you Jupiter and Saturn, for he is your Father, as much in death as in need. There comes my first-born Son, Saturn <term>♄</term>, I wish to speak with him.

<summary>Mercury describes its presence in all metals, particularly gold, and explains that it must be captured in its "young" or "green" state to be effective. It identifies itself as the "Father" of Saturn (lead) and Jupiter (tin), inviting a dialogue with the former.</summary>

153 <column-break/> K 5

<meta>The Golden Lion</meta>

tell; but since you have questioned me so sharply about it, I shall present it all to you briefly. You know that I am your eldest Son, and that my years are somewhat more numerous than those of my Brothers: and I have also performed much more labor than they. First you appointed me as a <term>Farmer</term> <note>original: Landman; signifying the "tilling" or initial preparation of the base mineral matter</note>; and after that as a <term>Water-man</term> <note>original: Waterman; likely referring to the stage of dissolution or the "washing" of metals</note>; and wherever I go, they hate me: whereas it goes quite differently with my Brothers, who through me are washed of their impurities and defects, which they still retained and brought with them out of the belly of the earth. I confess that I am held in contempt among them, especially by <term>Mars</term> <note>Iron</note> and <term>Venus</term> <note>Copper</note>; but in power I am the master of them all: for with my breath I cause them all to perish, and this is my water, which is not seen: and this is a dead body without spirit, which can then be awakened again by your spirit, Father <term>Mercury</term> <note>original: ☿</note>. My body is as cold as ice, my hair is grey, and my beard white like snow; should your heart not then take pity on me? Therefore all my Brothers say that I am marked with the mark of <term>Cain</term>, who struck his younger brother dead, for which he was hated by the entire world.

I, <term>Saturn</term> <note>original: ♄; the metal Lead</note>, am the highest among all my brothers in the Firmament of Heaven; without me

no respect: for when I am angry, then my flood of water comes, and I make my brothers bathe and swim; those who can then save themselves from my flood, I make into Kings and Queens over my kingdom, which is my Salt, and the <term>Sal Armoniac</term> <note>original: Sal Armoniac; ammonium chloride. In alchemy, this salt was used to "open" or dissolve metals, acting as a transformative agent.</note> among the Ancient Sages. Am I not earth and air? What more could one desire of me? And although my brothers then despise and disdain me, I am nonetheless the one through whom they are all examined <note>or "assayed"; a reference to the process of cupellation where lead is used to purify gold and silver.</note>; and whoever can endure these my tests is loved by the whole world, before whom all men must bow, and often risk their life and limb in heavy wars by land and by water, where <term>Neptune</term> <note>The Roman god of the sea; here signifying the alchemical "sea" or the dangerous stage of liquid dissolution.</note> dominates.

Whoever wishes to have my company must lay me in bed, with my Mother <note>Likely referring to the "Materia Prima" or the alchemical Mercury.</note> on one side and my Brother on the other, or all of us together: then I shall be able to serve the World, and they shall be able to honor me as a Lord.

<summary>Saturn (Lead) describes himself as a transformative "flood" and the "Sal Armoniac" of the ancients, explaining that while he is despised, he is the essential judge and purifier of the other metals. # The Golden Lion, #
<meta>catchword: I</meta>

[image-desc: A faint, watermark-like or bleed-through illustration is visible behind the text, depicting a seated figure, possibly a king or a personification, holding a scepter or staff.]

I must indeed confess that my body has two flaws, namely being cold and moist; and furthermore I am clothed with much unclean <term>Earth</term> <note>original: ♁; the symbol for Antimony or Earth. Here it represents the "dross" or crude mineral impurities that cling to the metal lead.</note>, with which I come to spoil everything: and therefore this <term>Earth</term> is regarded as a smoke <note>original: rook; likely referring to the volatile fumes or "soot" released when heating lead ore.</note>, which is very often poorly misused.

The moisture is my Salt, which you now behold, and it is clothed with a blue coat <note>Lead was traditionally associated with a dull blue or grey color in the alchemical "rainbow" of metals.</note> full of smoke and vapor; so that no one in the world can strip me of it, except only you, Father <term>Mercury</term> <note>original: ☿; the symbol for Mercury (Quicksilver). In alchemy, Mercury is often viewed as the "parent" of all metals and the only substance capable of truly dissolving and purifying them.</note>. For although nature brought me forth in your belly, and though you are purer than we all, we still cannot give you the blame for our impurity. Therefore I pray that you will forgive me for what I have done wrong, and will help me so that my coat may indeed be stripped off, and I may then be gloriously received into your Kingdom, so that I might do good for evil, and give generously and kindly, instead of stealing and robbing as I do now. <note>Saturn/Lead was often called a "thief" because, during the process of cupellation, lead "steals" or absorbs the impurities of other metals, but can also "consume" the precious metals if the process is not controlled.</note>

Over there I see my Brother <term>Jupiter</term> <note>Jupiter is the alchemical name for the metal Tin.</note> approaching; what might he be able to bring against me? For I know that he will want to complain about me to you.

<summary>Saturn (representing Lead) confesses his impurities—coldness, moisture, and "unclean earth"—to Mercury, begging for purification so he can be transformed from a "thief" into a generous inhabitant of Mercury's kingdom.</summary>

# ...or the Vinegar of the Sages. #

[An alchemical woodcut illustration depicting a male figure representing Jupiter. He is partially draped in a cloth, walking through a landscape with mountains in the background and a large tree to the right. The figure has the astrological symbol for Jupiter (♃) on his chest. He holds a triangular vessel with a cross (the alchemical symbol for <term>Vinegar</term> <note>original: Acetum; symbol 🜖. To the left, the symbols for Antimony (♁) and Saturn (♄) are visible. To the right, near the tree, the symbol for Jupiter is combined with a cross and a circle.]

I, <term>Jupiter</term> <note>original: ♃; Jupiter is the alchemical personification of the metal Tin. He is often portrayed as an intermediate figure between the "base" metal Lead and the "noble" metal Silver.</note>, am a God of Peace; in me all my Brothers are hidden in a spiritual manner; for to me belongs the robe of the purple garment <note>Purple was the color of Roman emperors and, in alchemy, represents the high degree of heat and refinement just before the completion of the "Great Work."</note>, although I have been cast out too far from the Kingdom by you, too evil man <term>Saturn</term> <note>original: ♄; Saturn represents Lead. Jupiter and Saturn are often paired as "The Heavy Ones," but here Jupiter seeks to distance himself from the "impurity" and "malice" associated with lead.</note>, whom I no longer wish to acknowledge as a Brother, since you [have done] me so much...

<summary>Jupiter (representing Tin) introduces himself as a rightful heir to the alchemical "Kingdom" and a "God of Peace," while sharply rebuking Saturn (Lead) for causing his exile and corruption.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Jupiter’s (Tin’s) monologue from the previous page. ->De Goude Leeuw,<-
<note>original: "The Golden Lion"</note>

...much grief, when I dwell with you. Do you well know that the Scepter belongs to me, and that I shall cause a precious golden crown to be placed upon those who honor me, and shall clothe all the Brothers in a purple garment? <note>The "purple garment" refers to the final stage of the alchemical process, the *rubedo* or redness, which signifies the perfection of the Philosopher's Stone.</note> When I am embraced by my Mother <note>In alchemy, the "Mother" often refers to the primordial substance or the "Philosophical Mercury" that nurtures the growing metal.</note>, then my soul emerges a beautiful red, clothed in purple, with which the King must be adorned.

My treasures are found in England and in the East Indies; <note>England (specifically Cornwall) and the East Indies were the primary historical sources of tin, the metal associated with Jupiter.</note> but the water that I must cross is very much salted with you, O <term>Saturn</term> <note>original: ♄; Saturn/Lead.</note>, all of which you do through our Father <term>Mercury</term> <note>original: ☿; In alchemical theory, all metals were thought to be composed of two principles: Sulfur (the father) and Mercury (the mother), or sometimes Mercury is called the father of all metals.</note>, whom I may give the most blame, since he has incited you to inflict such torment upon me. For if our Father had not whispered that in your ear, you would not be able to accomplish the same, which is not unknown to you. If you take me into the <term>Fire</term> <note>original: △; the alchemical symbol for the element of Fire.</note>, then you go away, and I remain lying there as ashes, so that I can hardly come to a *body* <note>original: *Corpus*; the solid, metallic form of a substance.</note>.

If I come into the <term>Water</term> <note>original: ▽; the alchemical symbol for the element of Water.</note>, then I become a lime <note>original: *kalk*; a "calx" is the powdery oxide formed when a metal is roasted or dissolved.</note>, which also cannot be reduced to a *body*; what then do you want from me? But when I am thoroughly cooked in my own nature, then I am capable of much, even to adorn <term>Saturn</term> <note>original: ♄; Lead.</note> with a golden crown, which cannot well happen without me.

--- Page 199 ---
<meta>This page concludes the monologue of Jupiter (representing the metal Tin). In the previous pages, Jupiter defended his noble nature against the criticisms of Saturn (Lead) and Mercury. Here, he describes his physical properties and chemical composition before the narrator transitions to a broader discussion of the three primary metallic species used in the Great Work.</meta>

# ...of the Vinegar of the Wise.
<note>The "Vinegar of the Wise" (*Acetum Philosophicum*) refers to a specialized alchemical solvent or "acid" used to dissolve metals into their primary components.</note>

My entire body is nothing other than <term>Sulfur</term> <note>original: 🜍; in alchemy, Sulfur represents the active, combustible, and "soul" principle of a substance.</note>, which is surrounded by a Mercurial earth: and this one can perceive in me when I am dissected; and you can also hear it well when I am bent, for then I gnash my teeth, which occurs because there is not much Salt in me. <note>This is a famous physical property of tin known as the "tin cry" (Dutch: *tinnengans*). When a bar of tin is bent, the crystal structure reorders itself, producing a distinct crackling or "gnashing" sound. Alchemists attributed this to a lack of the "Salt" principle, which provides stability and silence to metals.</note> Therefore, do not forget me, for I wear a white and a purple garment. <note>White and purple (red) refer to the *albedo* and *rubedo* stages of alchemy, suggesting that Tin contains the potential for the highest perfection of the Stone.</note> I would tell you even more of my nature, but because it has already been partially explained in what preceded, it shall be unnecessary to add any more here. Thus Jupiter concluded his speech.

We shall now then take our leave of <term>Jupiter</term> <note>original: ♃; Tin</note>, <term>Saturn</term> <note>original: ♄; Lead</note>, and <term>Mercury</term> <note>original: ☿; Quicksilver</note>, which are the three species from which the great Stone of the Wise, or the <term>Philosopher's Stone</term> <note>original: *Lapis Philosophorum*</note> should be made. And whoever desires more proof of this, let him read the Holy Scripture throughout: but in Job, in the 28th chapter, one finds that he speaks thus: *Surely there is a vein for the silver, and a place for gold where they refine it. Iron is taken out of the earth; and copper is molten out of the stone; and in other places as well.* <note>The author cites Job 28:1–2 to provide biblical authority for the existence and transmutation of metals.</note>

The Philosophers have always held Gold and Silver to be the mature metals, and iron and copper to be unripe metals.

<summary>Jupiter concludes his monologue by describing the "cry of tin" as a sign of his sulfurous nature. The narrator then summarizes that Jupiter, Saturn, and Mercury are the three foundational materials for the Philosopher's Stone, citing the Book of Job as evidence.</summary>

<keywords>Jupiter, Tin, Sulfur, Cry of Tin, Tria Prima, Salt, Philosopher's Stone, Job 28, mature metals, unripe metals, Silver, Gold, Iron, Copper</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion following Jupiter's speech. # The Golden Lion,
<page-num>160</page-num>

...[species], which the Man of God <note>Referring to the biblical figure Job, whose book contains many references interpreted by alchemists as metallurgical secrets.</note> also does, placing Gold and Silver above, and the iron and copper below. From this it appears that <term>Saturn</term> <note>original: ♄; Lead</note>, <term>Mercury</term> <note>original: ☿; Quicksilver</note>, and <term>Jupiter</term> <note>original: ♃; Tin</note> are kept secret in the Holy Scripture by all the Sages: for as it has been made known to me, more hidden things can be found in Saturn, Mercury, and Jupiter than one person in a thousand could ever believe. Therefore <term>Paracelsus</term> <note>The influential Swiss-German alchemist and physician (1493–1541).</note> also joined these together and wrote these words: that from Mercury, Saturn, and Jupiter, <term>Gold</term> <note>original: ☉; the Sun</note> and <term>Silver</term> <note>original: ☽; the Moon</note> are very easily made, but very difficult to make from <term>Iron</term> <note>original: ♂; Mars</note> and <term>Copper</term> <note>original: ♀; Venus</note>. So that one can clearly see what Job meant when he mentions the iron and copper, and remains silent about Jupiter, Mercury, and Saturn: for he did not do this without reason, but he possessed such knowledge of God to well know that in those three was the first beginning of Iron, Copper, Gold, and Silver; and for this reason, he did not wish to explain them in his book.

Hier some might say, perhaps in the place where Job lived, no Saturn, Jupiter, or Mercury were known; but I give as answer: That seldom or almost never will Iron or copper be found where some signs of Saturn or Jupiter and Mercury would not also be found. Indeed, near the Silver and the Gold that is found in Europe, Iron and Saturn are also very commonly present; for none of these, Iron, and Copper, and Silver, shall ever [exist] without...

<meta>catchword: the</meta>

<summary>The author interprets the biblical Book of Job through an alchemical lens, arguing that Job intentionally omitted the names of Saturn, Mercury, and Jupiter because they contain the primordial "first beginning" of all other metals. He supports this by citing Paracelsus's claim that these three metals are the easiest starting materials for creating gold and silver.</summary>
<keywords>Book of Job, Paracelsus, transmutation, biblical alchemy, Saturn, Mercury, Jupiter, Gold, Silver, Iron, Copper, metallurgy</keywords>

--- Page 201 ---
<meta>This page continues from page 160, where the author began explaining that even base metals contain traces of gold. The catchword "niet" (not) from the previous page completes the sentence here, stating that metals cannot be "without any Gold within them."</meta>

->of the Vinegar of the Sages.<-
<page-num>161</page-num>

...not be without any Gold within them; yet one more than the other, which can be perceived through trials.

For if the Gold <note>original symbol: ☉</note> is greater than the iron, then the iron is destroyed by the Antimony <note>original symbol: ♃ reversed; in early chemistry, antimony was often represented by a modified Jupiter symbol and was used to "devour" base metals to purify gold</note>, which will much rather seize the iron than the Gold, whereby it takes away the Iron <note>original symbol: ♂</note> and lets the Gold fall into a <term>Regulus</term> <note>original: Konink (King); the pure metallic mass that settles at the bottom of a crucible after smelting</note>, so that one only needs to blow it off on a flat sherd; then the Gold is fine, purified from its earth and friend-holding common Metals. In this manner, it can also be done with Silver <note>original symbol: ☽</note>, which occurs through Lead <note>original symbol: ♄</note>, which is likewise placed on a sherd and brought before the blast of the bellows, and then driven so long until everything is fine.

Through Mercury <note>original symbol: ☿; Quicksilver</note>, Gold and Silver can also be separated when they lie small as dust; but in hard iron-stones or copper it does not happen so, but that must occur through the violence of common Fire <note>original symbol: △</note>. They are also separated by Water <note>original symbol: ▽; here likely referring to "strong waters" or corrosive acids</note> and other sharp wood-lyes, which cause the Minerals to separate from their earths.

Since I have then begun to speak of Mercury <note>original symbol: ☿</note>, Saturn <note>original symbol: ♄</note>, and Jupiter <note>original symbol: ♃</note>, I say further, as much as has been made known to me by a good Friend, that from these—Antimony <note>original symbol: ♃ reversed</note>, Gold <note>original symbol: ☉</note>, and the Sun <note>original symbol: ☼</note>—the <term>Philosopher's Stone</term> <note>original: Steen der Wysen</note> is to be made, just as I have indicated here before. # The Golden Lion,
<page-num>162</page-num>

...does not make wet: when from this Water an air is made, then it is no longer a common Mercury <note>original symbol: ☿</note>; and then from this air an earth is made again, then it is fixed <note>original: gefigeert; made stable and resistant to the heat of fire</note>.

Saturn <note>original symbol: ♄; Lead</note> is the Salt of the earth, or the coldness of the Earth, which is overcome by Jupiter <note>original symbol: ♃; Tin</note> and Mercury <note>original symbol: ☿</note>. *Paracelsus says that the Metals are brought forth from three things into one being, namely from* Mercury <note>original symbol: ☿</note>, Sulfur <note>original symbol: triangleup</note>, *and* Salt <note>original symbol: ⊖</note>; and from these he also teaches how to make the *Philosophers' Stone* <note>original: Lapis Philosophorum</note>. For he says; Mercury <note>original symbol: ☿</note> is a spirit, and Sulfur <note>original symbol: triangleup</note> is the *Soul* <note>original: Anima</note>, and Salt <note>original symbol: ⊖</note> he calls the *Body* <note>original: Corpus</note>, and so forth.

I say then, that the Salt is brought to light by Mercury <note>original symbol: ☿</note>, out of Saturn <note>original symbol: ♄</note>; for which reason it is called the earth, and that is then the Salt of the Earth, or the earthly Lunar <note>Silver-like</note> Salt, and out of Jupiter <note>original symbol: ♃</note> the earthly Solar <note>Gold-like</note> Salt, and Mercury <note>original symbol: ☿</note> is the water, through which they are all brought forth. Therefore Basilius <note>referring to Basilius Valentinus, a legendary 15th-century alchemist</note> and also Paracelsus say; that Mercury <note>original symbol: ☿</note> is all in all, about which enough can be read in their works. For the entire body of Saturn <note>original symbol: ♄</note> is almost Salt, yet very earthy, which is difficult to separate, except through Sublimation <note>the process of heating a substance until it becomes a vapor and then collecting it as a solid again to purify it</note>, without the addition of any other strange Salts. Mercury <note>original symbol: ☿</note> is a volatile Salt, which also makes others volatile.

The body of Jupiter <note>original symbol: ♃</note> is almost nothing but Sulfur <note>original symbol: triangleup</note>, yet not so *fixed* <note>original: fixum; stable under heat</note> as Sol <note>original symbol: ☉; Gold</note>.

Mars <note>original symbol: ♂; Iron</note> has much Sulfur <note>original symbol: triangleup</note>, but very impure.

<summary>This page explains the "Tria Prima" (Mercury, Sulfur, and Salt) as the fundamental components of all metals according to Paracelsus and Basilius Valentinus. It details how specific metals like Lead, Tin, and Iron are composed of these elements in varying degrees of purity and stability.</summary>

--- Page 203 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion from page 162, moving from the philosophical nature of the three principles (Mercury, Sulfur, Salt) to practical laboratory instructions for extracting the "Soul" of metals and purifying gold using antimony.</meta>

<page-num>163</page-num>
<header>or the Vinegar of the Sages.</header>
<sig>L 2</sig>
<meta>catchword: heeft (has)</meta>

Copper <note>original symbol: ♀</note> has a great deal of Salt, and its Antimony <note>original symbol: ♵; here referring to the crude mineral ore</note> is likewise impure, so that not much can be accomplished with Iron <note>original symbol: ♂</note> and Copper <note>original symbol: ♀</note> alone. But if one wished to have the best *Soul* <note>original: Anima; the essential, transformative essence of a substance</note> from both of these, it can indeed be achieved by means of Sal Ammoniac <note>original symbol: 🜔; ammonium chloride, used as a flux</note>, though many difficulties are attached to it. One can also do this with Vitriol <note>original symbol: ⊕; a sulfate mineral used to create strong acids</note>, but that also involves much labor.

Now the easiest way that I know must be done through Antimony <note>original symbol: ♁</note>; if one makes a Regulus <note>a metallic mass that settles at the bottom of a crucible during smelting</note> from these two, then one obtains the true Mercury <note>original symbol: ☿</note> and the *Soul* <note>original: Anima</note> of Iron <note>original symbol: ♂</note> and Copper <note>original symbol: ♀</note>, which was discussed previously where I taught how to make the Regulus of Mars and Venus.

### ->To make a Carbuncle-stone out of Gold.<-

Gold <note>original symbol: ☉</note> is purified by Antimony <note>original symbol: ♁</note>; and when it has been cast through Antimony <note>original symbol: ♁</note> three times, according to the style of the Ancients, then in the end one obtains a Regulus. This Regulus having been placed in a crucible and well melted, one then takes a little Lead <note>original symbol: ♄</note>, putting that also into the crucible; and one then lets them melt together, thus one obtains a red oil on top; this shall be continually drawn off with an iron wire, and when it has become cold, then

Therefore *Basilius* <note>Basil Valentine, a legendary 15th-century alchemist</note> and also *Paracelsus* say that Mercury <note>original symbol: ☿</note> is all in all, of which enough can be read in their works. For the entire body of Saturn <note>original symbol: ♄</note> is almost entirely Salt, yet very earthy, which is difficult to separate, such as by sublimation <note>the process of turning a solid directly into vapor and back to solid to purify it</note>, without the addition of any other strange Salts. Mercury <note>original symbol: ☿</note> is a volatile Salt that also makes others volatile.

The body of Jupiter <note>original symbol: ♃</note> is almost nothing but Sulfur <note>original symbol: triangleup</note>, yet not as *fixed* <note>original: fixum; stable and non-volatile</note> as Gold <note>original symbol: ☉</note>.

Iron <note>original symbol: ♂</note> has much Sulfur <note>original symbol: triangleup</note>, but very impure.

One can obtain another calcination by means of this **Beast**, and put it into practice without the <term>sulphur</term> <note>original: 🜍; in alchemy, sulphur represents the principle of combustion and soul.</note>: for the <term>mercury</term> <note>original: ☿; representing the principle of fluidity and spirit.</note> is the highest <term>sulphur</term>, which not only consumes the metals into ashes, but even brings them to a <term>mercury</term> <note>This refers to the alchemical goal of "reducing" a solid metal back into its "mercurial" or liquid prime matter.</note>, about which enough can be read in the works of ***Paracelsus*** and ***Basilius*** <note>Referring to Basilius Valentinus, a legendary 15th-century alchemist whose writings were highly influential in the 17th century.</note> where he deals with <term>antimony</term> <note>original: Spijs-glas; a historical term for stibnite or metallic antimony, often used by alchemists to "purify" gold by "devouring" other metals.</note>, to which I wish to direct the seekers themselves, such as on page 18 of ***Basilius***.

When one would wish to have an ash from <term>tin</term> <note>original: ♃; the symbol for Jupiter.</note> and <term>lead</term> <note>original: ♄; the symbol for Saturn.</note>, then one must first melt both of them—both <term>tin</term> and <term>lead</term> in <term>equal parts</term> <note>original: ana; a Latin abbreviation used in recipes to indicate equal quantities of each ingredient.</note>—in a crucible over a gentle <term>fire</term> <note>original: 🜂</note>, and then take the crucible away or out of the <term>fire</term>, and let it cool down a little, until you begin to see that it wants to turn cold

I must confess, if one were to try to work with the <term>Antimony</term> <note>symbol: ♁; representing both the mineral and the "earth" of the philosophers.</note> literally according to the common method, and also take the Matter according to the Moon or the astrological signs, that one will then be deceived: for where <term>Antimony</term> is written, Gold must be understood; and where Silver is written, it must be <term>Tartar</term> <note>original: *Tartarum*. A salt found in wine casks, frequently used as a purifying agent in alchemy.</note>; where <term>Venus</term> <note>Usually the symbol for copper.</note> is written, there must be Urine or its Salt; and where they speak of <term>Saturn</term> <note>symbol: ♄; usually the symbol for lead.</note>, there is the Black Raven <note>original: *swarte Rave*. An alchemical code for the "nigredo" or blackening stage, representing the death and decomposition of the base matter before its rebirth.</note>.

Man loves **Wine**, and **Gold** loves **Mercury**—that is to say, man. Where is there more or better **Mercury** than in man? And there is also no greater <term>Fire<note>original: △</note></term> than in the human stomach, which may rightly be called an **Ostrich’s stomach** <note>This was a common early modern idiom for a powerful digestion. It was widely believed that ostriches could eat and digest metal.</note>.

Some people say that there is a bird that can digest iron, and I believe that our stomach can do the same better than that of a bird; for man, being created in God’s image, is far superior to a useless bird.

To explain the reason for this, I shall now make known in what manner this **Ostrich** bird digests iron, which I have seen for myself in the **Indies**; where the people took small pieces of old iron and gave them into its beak, which it then swallowed into its crop, and by morning it had passed through the anus again: this is done so often until the iron is entirely consumed. Yet the bird does not do this itself; rather, the iron is devoured by its own <term>Salt<note>original: 🜔</note></term>; for the **Vitriolic** <note>Vitriol refers to a class of chemical compounds (sulfates) known for their corrosive properties. ### or the Vinegar of the Sages.
<meta>catchword: these</meta>

**Oil** produced from the **Wine**, which was very heavy and beautiful of luster, just as any gold might ever be; yet there was no **animal nature** <note>In alchemy, "animal nature" refers to the vital spirit or "soul" of a substance necessary for it to "grow" into gold or provide life-extending properties.</note> present, so that I obtained nothing more than a wonderful **Medicine**. Now many might think to themselves: if I know the **Matter** <note>The "Matter" is the raw starting material used at the beginning of the alchemical process.</note>, then I must also know the manual techniques, or else I could not give a correct judgment of it. But I say, that some techniques are well known to me, but not yet all; regarding which I believe I have many companions in this matter.

A farmer knows stone and lime well, but that does not make him a **Master** to bring them together into a building; and *Basilius* <note>Referring to Basilius Valentinus, a legendary 15th-century alchemist famous for his "Twelve Keys."</note> speaks of this in the **Second Key**, page 24, and also in the **Fourth Key**, where one can read of these things and especially of the lime.

# De Goude Leeuw,
<note>original: "De Goude Leeuw" — The Golden Lion, a common alchemical title referring to the perfected state of matter or Gold.</note>

**This** is also the cause of the resurrection, and of a new life. This operation occurs by itself, but the other occurs by force, which is called ***Calcination*** <note>The process of heating a substance to high temperatures to drive off volatile matter or to oxidize it; here it refers to the purification of the "raw body."</note>, of the raw body or the raw juice; just as I have related before, that there is no better **ostrich stomach** <note>original: "Struifche maeg" — An alchemical metaphor for a powerful digestive heat or a specialized vessel capable of breaking down the toughest materials, much like the ostrich was once thought to digest iron.</note> to be found than that of man, in order to calcine the **Wine** therein; of which there shall now be enough said: he who wishes to know more of it may read further about it in other more long-winded writers. I cannot write further than I have experienced in **Nature**; and he who does not do so must be a **Liar**, and the truth shall never dwell with him.

The **imperfect metals** <note>Metals such as lead, tin, copper, and iron, which alchemists sought to "perfect" into gold.</note> may well be considered a bag of lies; for if one dissects <note>original: "ontleed" — to chemically analyze or break down into constituent parts.</note> them in the common way, you shall find nothing but lies. Therefore the **Philosophers** say: our <term>Venus (♀)</term> <note>symbol for Copper</note> is not common, our <term>Mars (♂)</term> <note>symbol for Iron</note> is not common: and so they say further of all the imperfect metals.

The **Old Sages** <note>original: "Oude Wyfen" — literally "Old Wives," but in this alchemical context it refers to the ancient authorities or sages.</note> say that their **Gold** is alive, as well as their <term>Moon (☽)</term> <note>symbol for Silver</note>, namely their **water**, which they take for the <term>Sun (☉)</term> <note>symbol for Gold</note>, as was taught in the previous verses. And this I have drawn from ***Rhumelius*** <note>Johann Pharamund Rhumelius (1597–1661), a noted German physician and alchemist.</note>, as one can read on page 253 of his work: indeed, in those few words he speaks more than enough.

### Concerning the Lion from the North.

I, Theophrastus, shall not be left in my grave, but men shall lay me out from my grave again toward the East; and I tell you that three great treasures are hidden: one in Meduno <note>original: "Meyden"; likely referring to Meduno or a similar locale in the Friuli region of Italy.</note> in Friuli <note>A historical region in northeastern Italy.</note>; the second between Swabia <note>A cultural and linguistic region in southwestern Germany.</note> and Bavaria—the specific place I do not name, in order to prevent much evil and great bloodshed; the third between France and Spain. # ...of the Vinegar of the Sages.

The Red Lion and the Green Lion are one; and that the Red Lion is born from the Green Lion, one reads in *Basilius* <note>Referring to Basilius Valentinus, a legendary 15th-century alchemist and author of "The Twelve Keys."</note>, in his *Repetitions of the Great Stone of the Sages*, pages 68 and 69, where you can see such things clearly enough. And to give us even better satisfaction, he has presented us with a <term>Regulus</term> <note>The metallic part of a mineral that sinks to the bottom during smelting; in alchemy, often associated with Antimony.</note>, which he calls the Salt of the Philosophers, page 69, where he says: *The Salt makes fixed and volatile, which is afterwards brought into its <term>Mercury</term> <note>original: ☿; the fluid, transformative principle of matter.</note>, or ordered and prepared.* Therefore he says that the <term>Tartar</term> <note>original: "Wijnsteen"; the crystalline sediment found in wine casks, used as a powerful flux or "secret fire" in alchemical work.</note> has two natures: one makes volatile, and the other fixes, of which enough can be read in that entire chapter, page 72. In that same place he says that the *spirit of Tartar* and the *spirit of Urine* <note>Ammonia-rich distillate used as a solvent.</note> possess great power; and to that he adds, *that he had spoken as a philosopher*. Thus I will conclude with him then, and say once more that Wine, Man, and Gold are everything in everything.

He also speaks of being born twice from the <term>Mercury</term> <note>original symbol: ☿</note>, as <term>Antimony</term>, <term>Antimony</term>, <term>Star</term> <note>original symbols: ♁, ♁, ✳; This refers to the "Star Regulus of Antimony," a crystalline form of the metal that exhibits a star-like pattern on its surface, highly prized by alchemists as a "sign" of the matter's readiness.</note>. And in the meaning of these signs, as far as I can perceive, lies the whole Art of obtaining the Salt or the Vinegar of the Sages.

Previously I have taught how one can extract the <term>Anima</term> <note>The "soul" or essential oily essence of a substance, believed to contain its medicinal and transformative power.</note> of <term>Antimony</term> <note>original symbol: ♁; a brittle, silvery-grey metalloid highly prized by alchemists for its purifying properties.</note> through <term>Vinegar</term> <note>original symbol: ☩; often referring to "Philosophical Vinegar," a potent acidic solvent used to break down minerals.</note>, and obtain it sweet; yet now I must say in brief that this Oil is made sweet with <term>Iron</term> <note>original symbol: ♂; the metal associated with the planet Mars.</note>, when it has stood to ferment with it for a long time, and it is then as sweet as any thing of smell and taste. When this Oil is boiled down to a second part <note>Likely meaning reduced by half or to a specific density.</note>, and then allowed to grow cold, one obtains beautiful green stones that are as sweet as sugar: yet before one joins that Oil with <term>Iron</term> <note>original symbol: ♂</note>, there must be two parts <term>Water</term> <note>original symbol: ▽</note> present, then one obtains the correct ferment; for without it having fermented, it shall not become sweet.

When you now have the green stone, you can <term>calcine</term> <note>A process of heating a substance to high temperatures in the presence of air to oxidize it or change its physical state.</note> it in two ways in order to open its body; and this occurs in the flaming <term>Fire</term> <note>original symbol: △</note>, in a <term>Reverberatory furnace</term> <note>original: "Reverbercer-oven"; a furnace where the heat is reflected (reverberated) from the roof onto the material, preventing direct contact with the fuel.</note>, just as can be seen in the figure in the *Cabinet of Minerals*, which is constructed with two chimneys. The other calcination occurs above the <term>Fire</term> <note>original symbol: △</note>, on an earthen or iron plate; or each may take what is most convenient for him. The calcination in the open <term>Fire</term> <note>original symbol: △</note> makes the Stones blood-red, which you obtained from the Oil.

# The Golden Lion,
->R. 4.<-

Oil is made by means of the <term>Water of the Sun</term> <note>original: *Aqua* ☉; likely refers to *Aqua Regia*, a potent mixture of acids capable of dissolving gold, the metal associated with the Sun</note>. When the dust of <term>Antimony</term> <note>original symbol: ♁; a brittle, silvery-gray metalloid that was a primary subject of study for alchemists seeking the "Philosopher's Stone"</note> is sprinkled into it, it becomes red, but it is then very fiery upon the tongue. These act very gently upon one another and give off a sulfurous stench. After they have stood together for 6 or 7 days, one passes this <term>Matter</term> <note>original: *Materia*</note> through a felt-paper <note>an early form of filter paper used to strain solids from liquids</note>, which will be dark red. This water, now distilled through the felt-paper, may rightly be called a **Red Lion** <note>a symbolic term in alchemy often representing the final stage of a process or a powerful, corrosive solvent</note>; regarding which much could be written.

### of the Vinegar of the Sages.

Take **Antimony** <note>original symbol: ♁; a metalloid used as a primary material in alchemical transformations</note>. Distill it according to common practice; and the spirit that comes over shall be much stronger at the end of the distillation than at the beginning, which some mistakenly consider to be a <term>phlegm</term> <note>original: *flegma*; usually refers to a watery, tasteless, and medically "cold" byproduct of distillation thought to have no value</note>, but wrongly so; for in the first part, the Spirit of Wine comes over—the **Fire** <note>original symbol: △</note> that is in the Wine, which shall never <term>rot</term> <note>original: *putrificeren*; to undergo alchemical decay or fermentation</note> for all eternity, but shall always raise itself above the earth. In the latter part comes the **Vinegar** <note>original symbol: 🜔</note>, which is nothing other than the **Mercury** <note>original symbol: ☿</note> and the **Antimony** <note>original symbol: ♁</note> that was in the Wine, and which has created a separation through its acidification.

Once the Vinegar is thus prepared, one has it ready to separate the **Sulfur** <note>original symbol: 🜍</note> and **Salt**.

This Vinegar is then mixed with good distilled **Spirit** <note>original symbol: 🜈</note> of Brandy—specifically, two parts Brandy to three parts Vinegar—then the water is ready to separate the Sulfur from the Salt; namely, that which is made from the white Vinegar through the **Nitre-Water** <note>original: *Aqua* ①; likely *Aqua Fortis* or nitric acid, often symbolized by a circle with a cross or a specific number in this manuscript’s system</note>, which I have taught how to make in the *Cabinet of Minerals*; though in this treatise it is also partly taught: the preparation is by means of burning charcoal, and the <term>dissolution</term> <note>original: *ſolvering*; the process of a solid being dissolved into a liquid state</note> follows afterward in the air.

When the Vinegar then enters therein, it draws that Salt to itself, and the Sulfur remains separately on the bottom. Afterward the Vinegar

When this has been done, one dissolves in that Vinegar as much <term>Sal Armoniac</term> <note>original symbol: 🜹</note> as it will hold, and afterwards filters it through a felt paper; then it is bright and clear.

### of the Vinegar of the Sages.

In this <term>Sal Armoniac</term> <note>original symbol: ⚹; ammonium chloride</note> thus sharpened with <term>Saltpeter</term> <note>original symbol: 🜔; potassium nitrate</note>, one causes the fine <term>Sulfur</term> <note>original symbol: 🜍; the combustible or "soul" principle of a metal</note> to separate from the <term>Copper</term> <note>original symbol: 🜚</note>, and it then dissolves blood-red: this must be distilled through a felt paper, and so it is ready to be united with its Salt, as was told before.

This <term>Sulfur</term> <note>original symbol: 🜍</note> taken together thus with its Salt must be distilled up to three times; then the <term>Sulfur</term> <note>original symbol: 🜍</note> remains with its body; that shall thereafter be taken out of the flask, and it is [produced] from the green, very fiery on the tongue; and laid upon a glass table in a damp cellar, it runs into a yellow Oil, which is very pleasant of taste. *NB.* <note>Latin: *nota bene*; note well</note> To bring this yellow fiery Oil to a blood-red Oil, one shall proceed in the following manner. Afterwards take another receiver, which is dry, and drive it [apply heat] so long until all the <term>Saltpeter</term> <note>original symbol: 🜔</note> is sublimated <note>the process where a solid turns into gas and back to solid without becoming liquid</note>; and this is done with great <term>Fire</term> <note>original symbol: 🜂</note>. *Basilius* <note>Basil Valentine, a legendary 15th-century alchemist and author whose works emphasize the spiritual nature of chemistry</note> speaks of the parts of the small World <note>the human body, viewed as a miniature reflection of the universe</note>, in his book the *Microcosm* <note>original: *Microcosmo*</note>, page 104. *The substances and form of this Microcosm are made from earth and water.* Here he teaches us what we are made of, namely from earth and water: by these, man is also sustained; and from these same things the Metals and Minerals are brought forth, indeed all things in the entire World; of which *Sendivogius* <note>Michael Sendivogius (1566–1636), a famous Polish alchemist</note> recounts, as has also been cited throughout this book, and will be further explained in the Closing Argument. *Basilius* calls this spirit on page 106 the <term>Vital Force</term> <note>original: *Vitalis*; the life-giving essence</note>, which has life, or gives life, and that is the <term>Spirit</term> <note>symbol: 🜔; the volatile, life-giving essence of a substance</note> that is found in Humans, as well as in the Metals and in Wine.

**NB.** The Salt partly gives fine <term>Spirit</term> <note>symbol: 🜔</note> for the maintenance of human blood; for in the Salt that comes from man <note>referring to salts extracted from human waste or sweat</note>, there resides a spirit that far surpasses other balms. Therefore it should not seem strange to us that *Paracelsus* <note>Theophrastus Paracelsus (1493–1541), a revolutionary physician who believed that "filth" and waste products contained powerful medicinal virtues</note> brought "peasants' dung" to light and showed the balm of health to be contained within it. # The Golden Lion,
<meta>catchword: The</meta>

...since one may consider man as a **little World** <note>The "Microcosm"—the idea that the human body reflects the entire universe and contains all its elements</note>, so his **Salt** is of a **Mineral** nature, which has fellowship with the **Metals** and **Minerals**. For the **Salt** made from his **Urine** has the power to take hold of and dissolve **Gold** <term>☉</term> <note>original symbol: ☉; the alchemical sign for the sun and the metal gold</note>. The Ancients have called this Salt **Sal Ammoniac** <term>⚹</term> <note>original symbol: ⚹; a volatile salt used by alchemists to facilitate chemical reactions and dissolve substances</note>: not because it is found in that state, but because it is brought forth from the **red earth** and even consumed by us before it can become such a Salt or **Salt** <term>⦵</term> <note>original symbol: ⦵; Salt is one of the three "Primes" of alchemy, representing the body or principle of fixity</note>. For this Salt <term>⦵</term> is first a **Green Lion** <note>An alchemical metaphor for a powerful, raw acidic solvent or unrefined matter</note>, and through man it thereafter becomes a **Golden** one; yet others have also written of this, for which reason I shall let it rest here.

One can bring the **Urine** into fermentation through a Salt of **Antimony** <term>♁</term> <note>original symbol: ♁; a metalloid used in alchemy both for purifying gold and for creating powerful medicines</note>; as also through **Antimony** <term>♁</term>, and everything that is contrary to it; indeed, all common **Minerals** and **Salts** struggle against it. But enough of this for this time.

I intend to write further after this on the use of the ***Anima*** <note>Latin: "Soul." In alchemy, this refers to the quintessence or the essential "tincture" extracted from a substance</note> of the **Metals** and **Salts**, which I have previously taught how to extract; but he who has progressed so far can first administer the **red soul** of the same in a very small **dose** <note>original: *Dosis*</note>, and through experience gradually proceed further. He who wishes to know more about this can read of it in ***Paracelsus*** <note>Philippus Aureolus Theophrastus Bombastus von Hohenheim (1493–1541), a famous physician and alchemist</note> and ***Basilius*** <note>Basil Valentine, the supposed author of many influential alchemical texts</note>, where everyone shall be able to find satisfaction.

### Common Sal Ammoniac is made as follows.

℞. <note>The symbol ℞ stands for the Latin word *recipe*, meaning "take."</note> One pound of **Sal Ammoniac** <term>🜔</term> <note>original symbol: 🜔; used here for Sal Ammoniac or common salt</note>, and twice as much **soot** from the chimney. These are boiled together with human **urine** for 4 or 5 days, always adding fresh urine until everything becomes like a black porridge. This must then be dried over a gentle **fire** <term>🜂</term> <note>original symbol: 🜂; represents the element of fire or the application of heat</note>, and afterwards dissolved again in fresh urine, and filtered through felt-paper <note>a thick paper or cloth used to strain out impurities</note>, and then dried once more over a gentle **fire** <term>🜂</term>; it is then ready to be **sublimated** <note>the process of heating a substance so it turns into vapor and then solidifies again as a purified crust</note> with common salt, so that the body becomes pure for use in this **Art** <note>a standard term for the practice of Alchemy</note>.

A **Sal Ammoniac** <term>⚹</term> <note>original symbol: ⚹; another common symbol for Sal Ammoniac, a volatile salt used for dissolving metals</note> is also made from human **blood** and **urine**; but common salt must be added to it, in this manner.

℞. One pound of blood, 2 pounds of common salt, 7 or 8 pounds of clean **water** <term>🜄</term> <note>original symbol: 🜄; represents the element of water</note>; mix all of these together, and then dry over a gentle **fire** <term>🜂</term>, and it is ready; however, it must sit for some time before it is boiled down, otherwise the **Salt** would not be good for use.

From the **urine** and *dung* <note>original: *Stercus*; Latin for excrement or manure</note> mixed together, one obtains a very wonderful salt, both vola- <note>The text cuts off here at the catchword "vluch-," likely for "vluchtig" or volatile</note>

When the **Mercury** <term>☿</term> is thus bound, it will have the color of **Gold** <term>☉</term> <note>original symbol: ☉; referred to by the Latin name *Sol* (the Sun)</note>, though it is not stable in the **fire** <term>△</term> <note>original symbol: △; referred to by the Latin name *Ignis*</note> upon the **cupel** <note>a shallow, porous container used in assaying to separate precious metals from base metals; if a substance is not "stable" here, it indicates it is not true gold</note>: but if one lets it melt in a crucible, one can easily cast a ring from it. The process is in the following manner.

℞ <note>original: *Recipe*; the standard medical and alchemical command meaning "Take"</note> One pound of **Mercury** <term>☿</term>, one pound of **Verdigris** <note>original: *Spaens-groen*; a green pigment formed by the action of acetic acid on copper</note>, half a pound of **Common Salt** <note>original: *Sout Commum*</note>, half a pound of **Sal Ammoniac** <term>🜔</term> <note>original: *Salmiak*</note>, half a pitcher of urine, and half a pint of vinegar. 

When these above-written ingredients are all pounded, they shall be put into the vinegar and the urine, and boiled in an iron pan over a gentle **fire** <term>△</term>. During the boiling, this shall be constantly stirred with a stick, otherwise the **Mercury** <term>☿</term> would not allow itself to be bound. This boiling must last an entire day, and as the moisture evaporates from it, one shall always pour new **Sal Ammoniac** <term>🜔</term> and urine into it. When this **Mercury** <term>☿</term> has been so boiled, one shall take that material out of the pan, and into sweet clean **water** <term>🜄</term> <note>original symbol: 🜄; referred to by the Latin name *Aqua*</note> pour—

# Concluding Argument.

THE Ancient Sages say that the Art shall make its beginning where Nature has left off, namely in the perfect metals, such as <term>Gold (☉)</term> and <term>Silver (☽)</term>; but in the others, such as <term>Iron (♂)</term>, <term>Copper (♀)</term>, <term>Tin (♃)</term>, and <term>Lead (♄)</term>, Nature has not finished. This is by reason of the Womb, namely the cold and raw air, which Nature has not been able to warm, whereby she has not been able to perform her work. Read on this point the Maxim of *Cosmopolite* <note>original: "Cosmopolite" refers to Michael Sendivogius (1566–1636), a famous Polish alchemist. His "Novum Lumen Chymicum" was a standard text for the "Sons of Art" (alchemists).</note>, pages 17 and 18, which reads thus: *Where Nature stops—to wit, in the metallic bodies that are perfect before our eyes—there one must begin the Art again.* And to show with what we must make our beginning, he says on page 17: *The seed <note>The term "sperma" or "seed" refers to the generative principle within matter; alchemists believed all metals grew from a single source in the earth.</note> of the metals is no different from the seed of all things in the world, and that is a moist vapor.*

By this we are also warned, and sufficiently made known, *That <term>Lead (♄)</term> and <term>Gold (☉)</term> would each have their own particular seed, and that every metal has its own particular seed; but this opinion is vain, for there is but one single seed, as well in <term>Lead (♄)</term>, as in Gold, in Silver,*

<summary>This page argues that the alchemist's work begins where nature's process ends, specifically by using "perfect" metals (gold and silver) as a starting point. It cites the alchemist Cosmopolite to explain that all metals share a single, universal "seed" or moist vapor, rather than having unique origins.</summary>

<keywords>alchemy, gold, silver, planetary symbols, Michael Sendivogius, Cosmopolite, metallic seed, perfection of metals, Vinegar of the Sages</keywords>

->The Golden Lion,<-
<meta>catchword: Salt</meta>

and in the Iron. So that this teaching makes us recognize where we should seek the <term>seed <note>original: "sperma". In alchemy, this refers to the generative essence or "sperm" of a metal, believed to contain its life-force.</note></term>, namely not in Lead <note>original symbol: ♄ (Saturn)</note>, but in Gold; for what one sows, that shall also be reaped: and *Basilius* <note>Basil Valentine, a legendary 15th-century Benedictine monk and alchemist.</note> says this also in his discourse, which I have cited here before.

*Cosmopolite* <note>Michael Sendivogius (1566–1636), who wrote under the pseudonym "Cosmopolite."</note> says of *Count Bernard* <note>Bernard of Trevisan (1406–1490), an Italian alchemist who spent his life and fortune seeking the Philosopher's Stone.</note>, that he speaks of this seed when he presents the four Elements; yet his words are obscure. This can now be read in his book on page 139: *But I tell you of this Proposition in sum, that all Metals shall be generated in the <term>First Matter <note>original: "prima Materia". The formless base of all substance that alchemists sought to isolate and refine.</note></term>, and that is the Mercury and Sulfur <note>original symbol: 🜍</note>, which have sprouted from the 4 Elements, been generated, and have become Sulfur <note>original symbol: 🜍</note> and Mercury <note>original symbol: ☿</note>.*

The Philosophers write that the seed or sperm is in the Metals; but of the field in which such seed shall be cast, they do not write. Some call it, though by a veiled name, the Mercury of the Metals; but that is false. In *Sendivogius* one reads on page 62 that this field in which one shall cast his seed, *is the Salt Alkali, which the Philosophers have called <term>Sal Ammoniac <note>original: "Sal Armoniacum". # The Golden Lion #

the Metallic Nature that is in the <term>Mercury <note>original symbol: ☿</note></term>; and in what manner that is put to work, you may read in my *Cabinet of Minerals*. If now the <term>Mercury <note>☿</note></term> makes the Silver heavier, then there must necessarily be a Mineral nature in the Wine. Therefore the Philosophers say that God has made three things in the world that correspond in nature: namely, Man, Gold, and Wine. And since the Sages have written much about this, I may well relate what is known to me: that the Wine, the Vinegar, and the <term>Brandy <note>original: 'Brandewijn'; literally 'burnt wine,' referring to distilled spirits or aqua vitae</note></term>, along with its <term>stone <note>original: 'fynen Steen'; likely referring to Tartar, the potassium bitartrate crystals that deposit in wine casks, considered a "salt" by alchemists</note></term>, are the four things that almost contain the whole of nature within themselves.

There is no better moisture in the whole world than Wine, and therefore its stone has no equal in the whole world; for when it comes to man, it makes him volatile <note>In an alchemical sense, this suggests making the spirit active or "flighty," often a metaphor for intoxication or spiritual elevation</note>; and when it comes into the <term>Antimony <note>original symbol: ♁; a mineral used to purify gold and seen as a "mask" for the hidden spirit</note></term>, it fixes <note>To "fix" a substance means to transform it from a volatile, evaporating state into a solid, stable form that can withstand fire</note> it: just as *Basilius* <note>Basil Valentine, a legendary 15th-century alchemist</note> teaches in the chapter on <term>Mercury <note>☿</note></term> and Vinegar, which anyone can read.

And to have even more information regarding the Vinegar of the Sages, you may see the *Rosarium* in the *Turba* <note>The *Turba Philosophorum* (Assembly of the Philosophers) is one of the oldest Latin alchemical texts; the *Rosarium* refers to a specific section or the related text *Rosarium Philosophorum*</note>, page 258, which says thus: ***The Stone that comes into the Work has proceeded from one thing, and it is found in all places; indeed, the poor have this Stone as well as the rich:***

# The Golden Lion

it grows out of flesh and blood, and very precious is he who knows it.

The Philosopher Mirigo <note>A speaker appearing in the *Turba Philosophorum*, one of the oldest Latin alchemical texts</note> speaks in *The Assembly* <note>original: "Turba [Philosophorum]"</note> page 259: *Even though our Stone is trodden underfoot by both humans and other cattle, nevertheless the Wise hold it very dear, because of its usefulness for mankind.*

It is not said here that the entire Matter of the Stone is found within man, but this must be understood to mean that a part of it is in man—namely, that which creates the Astral <note>Referring to the "Astral Spirit" or "Star-fire," a celestial influence believed to animate physical matter</note> and the "Feathers or Plumes of the Eagle" <note>An alchemical metaphor for the volatile white vapors or "spirit" released during sublimation</note>, which I have often indicated in this book with the name of <term>Mercury <note>original symbol: ☿</note></term>. This is a Vegetable Stone <note>In alchemy, "Vegetable" refers to the principle of growth and life-force, often associated with spirits of wine or plants</note>, while that which comes from man is an Animal Stone; and when the <term>Sulphur <note>original symbol: 🜍</note></term> and the <term>Mercury <note>☿</note></term> of Gold are added to it, then those three are two and no more <note>A paradox suggesting that the three ingredients fuse into a singular, unified substance</note>: yet you should know that the Salt which comes from man is much higher than the <term>Copper <note>original symbol: ♀; likely referring here to the "Vitriol of Venus" or a specific metallic salt discussed earlier</note></term> which was treated before.

### ...of the Vinegar of the Wise.

...has three cloaks <note>Likely referring to the three layers, shells, or envelopes of matter that conceal the inner essence</note> from which they have made this Stone of the Wise, and it is nonetheless brought forth from a single nature.

This can be seen on pages 20, 21, and 22 in the treatise by Mr. Snyders <note>Johannes de Monte-Snyder, a prominent 17th-century Dutch-German alchemist</note> titled *The Universal Medicine* <note>original: "Medicina universali"</note>. Therein he states on page 22 that Mercury improves the metals and makes them flexible. He also calls it the *Mineral field* <note>original: "Mineralischen akker"</note> wherein the seed of the metals is cast; and throughout the entire 11th chapter, dealing with the *Magical Elements* <note>original: "Elementis Magicis"</note>, he indicates that there are three Salts that belong to the work. Basil Valentine <note>original: "Basilius"; a legendary alchemical figure whose writings focus on antimony and the "Twelve Keys"</note> also makes mention of this in his explanation of the *Twelve Keys* <note>original: "12 Sleutels"</note>, page 202, with these words: *I have presented and described to you the correct dissolution of the three beginning principles, yet it does not happen so swiftly.* He also says that from two and three, one has come, yet in a single essence.

The reason I have added all these sayings here at the end is for the common benefit, as they all agree unanimously on the correct Matter <note>original: "Materia"; the "First Matter" or starting material of the Great Work</note>. I am able to know this well enough, even though I do not possess the Stone myself, as all these substances are sufficiently known to me,

**Mr. Snyders** <note>Johannes de Monte-Snyders, a prominent 17th-century alchemist known for his theories on the "universal salt"</note> speaks of this Salt on pages 37 and 38: *That this Salt is found in human blood, and especially in the human bladder, where it is most easily detected.* And since enough can be read about it in his writings, I wish to recommend him to the kind reader.

For unless the Salt is **putrefied** <note>original: "verrot"; the alchemical stage of "Putrefaction" where matter breaks down and turns black</note>, no separation of the fine earth can occur; and once the Salt is purified, then the Matter is ready, so that its joining requires little time. *Venus am I, bold and wild*
*As everyone can behold,*
<margin>♀ <note>The astronomical and alchemical symbol for Venus, representing the metal copper</note></margin> *I carry in my shield*

*To honor your Hermes,* <note>Referring to Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary father of alchemy; to "honor Hermes" is to practice the Hermetic Art correctly</note>

*Strip all the clothes off me*
*Through the Eagles' brave powers,* <note>The "Eagles" are alchemical metaphors for volatile substances or acidic spirits (like Sal Ammoniac) that "attack" and strip away the outward metallic form</note>
*Then you see the fairest Bride* <note>The "Bride" represents the purified substance or the "White Stone" in its nascent stage</note>
*To be held in high esteem in the blue.* <note>When copper is dissolved in acid, it turns from green to a brilliant blue; this transition was seen as a sign of purification</note>

*Clear as a bright Crystal*
*You discover my limbs,*
*To observe them all closely*
*You must devote much diligence.*

# The Golden Lion, or etc.

*He who then leads me into the Wine-house,* <note>In alchemical allegory, the "Wine-house" refers to the vessel where the "Philosophical Wine" or an acidic solvent (like spirit of wine or distilled vinegar) is used to dissolve the metallic body.</note>

# Explanation of the Symbols commonly used by the Alchemists, and now used here in this book.

☉, Gold <note>original: "Aurum, Sol"</note>.
☽, Silver <note>original: "Argentum, Luna"</note>.
♂, Iron <note>original: "Mars, Yfer"</note>.
♀, Copper <note>original: "Venus, Koper"</note>.
♃, Tin <note>original: "Jupiter, Tin"</note>.
♄, Lead <note>original: "Saturnus, Lood"</note>.
☿, Quicksilver <note>original: "Merkurius, Quiksilver"</note>.
🜊, Alum <note>original: "Aluin"; a mineral salt used as a mordant in dyeing and in various chemical purifications.</note>.
🜔, Saltpeter <note>original: "Salpeter"; potassium nitrate, a key ingredient in making strong mineral acids.</note>.
🜔, Common Salt <note>original: "Gemeen Sout"</note>.
🜖, Vitriol, Copper-red <note>original: "Vitriool, Koper-rood"; usually refers to metallic sulfates like iron or copper sulfate, which were foundational to alchemical experiments.</note>.
🜕, Rock Salt <note>original: "Sal Gemmæ, Steen-Sout"</note>.
🜀, Calcined Tartar <note>original: "Gecalcineerde Wijn-steen"; tartar that has been heated to a high temperature to create a white powder, often used as a flux or purifying agent.</note>.
🜄, Tartar, Raw Tartar <note>original: "Tartarum, Rouwe Wijn-steen"; the potassium bitartrate crust that forms naturally in wine barrels.</note>.
🜍, Sulfur <note>original: "Sulphur, Swavel"</note>.
🜹, Sal Ammoniac <note>original: "Sal Armoniac"; ammonium chloride, prized for its ability to help substances sublimate or rise in the flask.</note>.
🜺, Arsenic, Rat's bane <note>original: "Arsenicum, Rotte-kruit"; literally "rat-powder," a common historical name for arsenic when used as a pesticide.</note>.
♁, Antimony <note>original: "Antimonium"; a brittle semi-metal used to purify gold.</note>.
🜚, Verdigris, or Roman Vitriol <note>original: "Spaens Groen, of Roomsche Vitriool"; "Spanish Green" refers to the green patina formed on copper, while Roman Vitriol typically refers to blue copper sulfate.</note>.
🜊, Vinegar <note>original: "Azijn"</note>.
🜋, Distilled Vinegar <note>original: "Gedistilleerden Azijn"</note>.
🜄, Rainwater <note>original: "Regen-water"</note>.

<margin><image-desc>An inverted triangle symbol appears in the left margin.</image-desc> <term>Δ</term>, Water.</margin>

<term>🜅</term>, *Strong water* <note>original: "Aqua fortis, ſterk-water"; nitric acid, a powerful corrosive used by alchemists and goldsmiths to dissolve and refine metals.</note>.

<term>□</term>, *Body* <note>original: "Corpus, Gecalcineert Sout"; literally the "body" of a substance, here specifically referring to salt that has been reduced to a dry powder through high heat.</note>.

<term>♎</term>, *Spirit* <note>original: "Spiritus, Geeſt"; the volatile or gaseous essence of a substance that can be captured through distillation.</note>.

<term>☿</term>, *Calx* <note>original: "Kalk"; the crumbly, ash-like residue left behind after a metal or mineral has been thoroughly burnt or oxidized.</note>.

->LEAF<-

<meta>Following the alphabetical index which concluded with entries on the "Green Lion" and the medicinal properties of wine, this page serves as the formal title page for the treatise itself, transitioning from the reference guide to the core alchemical text.</meta>

# T H E
# Red Lion,
### OR THE SALT
### of the
### P H I L O S O P H E R S.

<note>The "Red Lion" (original: "Roode Leeuw") is a powerful alchemical symbol. While the "Green Lion" mentioned in the previous index often refers to a strong solvent or raw mineral, the Red Lion typically represents the final, perfected stage of the "Great Work"—the Philosopher's Stone or the Red Tincture capable of transmuting base metals into gold.</note>

<image-desc>An alchemical woodcut or engraving depicting two palm trees flanking a body of water. The tree on the left features a crescent moon in its fronds and the symbol for <term>Jupiter</term> <note>Symbol: ♃; in alchemy, this planetary symbol represents the metal tin.</note> on its trunk. The tree on the right features a sun with a face in its fronds and the symbol for <term>Saturn</term> <note>Symbol: ♄; in alchemy, this planetary symbol represents the metal lead, often considered the starting point of the work.</note> on its trunk. Various alchemical and planetary symbols (including <term>Mercury</term> <note>Symbol: ☿; representing quicksilver and the principle of volatility.</note>, <term>Venus</term> <note>Symbol: ♀; representing the metal copper.</note>, <term>Mars</term> <note>Symbol: ♂; representing the metal iron.</note>, <term>Antimony</term> <note>Symbol: ♁; a metalloid used as a purifying agent in the refining of gold.</note>, and <term>Salt</term> <note>Symbol: ⦵ or 🜔; representing the physical "body" or stability of a substance.</note>) are dispersed throughout the sky and water between the trees.</image-desc>

->Printed for the Author.<-

<summary>This is the title page of the alchemical treatise "The Red Lion," which identifies its primary subject as the "Salt of the Philosophers." The page features a symbolic woodcut illustrating the planetary and elemental relationships necessary for the "Great Work" of transmutation.</summary>
